Should You Forget IonQ and Buy These 2 Quantum Computing Stocks Instead?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

D-Wave Quantum and Rigetti are taking different approaches to developing quantum computers. Here's how their commercial progress and valuations stack up against IonQ's.

IonQ (IONQ +23.42%) may be a leading pure-play name in the quantum computing race, but it certainly isn't the only promising one. D-Wave Quantum (QBTS +18.01%) and Rigetti Computing (RGTI +19.29%) are also among the stronger picks in the space, and each brings distinctive technologies and growth opportunities to the table.

Could these two quantum stocks be better buys than IonQ right now?

D-Wave's biggest advantage is its focus on quantum annealing, a niche approach to the technology that is largely useful only for solving optimization and sampling problems. However, those types of problems come up frequently in a host of applications, from managing supply chains and logistics to materials science and chemistry. Customer adoption of its systems has been picking up, with more companies using D-Wave's quantum technology to improve their operations.

That's starting to show up in its financial results.

In August, the company delivered its Q2 numbers and reported that production applications accounted for a growing share of its quantum cloud computing revenue. It also expanded its commercial relationships during the quarter, including an agreement with AT&T to broaden the use of quantum computing in network operations.

Plus, with its acquisition of Quantum Circuits, the company has been developing a dual-platform strategy that combines its annealing approach with a more common gate-model quantum computing approach. That's important because gate-model systems have the potential to address a much broader range of problems in computing.

With that, D-Wave may appeal more to investors than IonQ. In fact, its technology is already being used in production applications, helping businesses optimize their operations. That gives it a more immediate path to monetization.

Of course, there are risks in owning a stock like D-Wave. Quantum annealing has its value, but it doesn't address every problem that general-purpose quantum computers could potentially solve. D-Wave is also still at an early stage of its journey toward a commercially viable product, so adoption still needs to translate into more sustainable revenue growth and profitability.

On top of that, with the stock trading at a price-to-sales (P/S) ratio of around 300, its valuation prices in a whole lot of hoped-for future growth. If the company doesn't deliver on those expectations, the stock could decline dramatically.

Still, the majority of Wall Street analysts covering the stock rate it a buy, with an average 12-month target price of $33.83, implying about 226% upside.

Meanwhile, another quantum stock is taking a unique approach to scaling quantum computers.

Rigetti offers a different opportunity: It's using superconducting qubits to build powerful, general-purpose quantum computers. It's also developing full-stack technology, spanning hardware, software, and the infrastructure needed to run quantum computers.

The company has also made meaningful progress expanding its commercial and research partnerships. In July, Rigetti announced an expanded collaboration with Hewlett Packard Enterprise and the Pittsburgh Supercomputing Center. The project involves deploying Rigetti's Novera quantum system, allowing researchers to explore how quantum processors can work alongside traditional supercomputers.

Rigetti is also working to expand its international footprint, including plans to invest in the U.K. and deliver systems to research institutions. These relationships could help drive adoption of its technology and create opportunities for future commercial deals.

If superconducting qubits become the leading approach to scaling up quantum computers to a commercially useful size, Rigetti could be one of the biggest beneficiaries. Its chiplet-based design, in-house manufacturing, and federal government backing also make it an interesting way to invest in the next generation of quantum hardware.

However, Rigetti's path to potential success will depend on improving processor performance, scaling its quantum systems without compromising quality, and securing commercial demand. As a result, the company faces significant execution risk.

Its valuation also is approaching 300 times sales, so it, too, will have to deliver on extremely high expectations to generate the sales and profits necessary to justify its current share price and produce additional returns. Still, like D-Wave, the majority of analysts covering Rigetti rate the stock a buy, with an average 12-month target price of $27.69, implying about 183% upside.

Each company offers a different way to bet on how quantum computing will evolve, and each has its own path to growth. D-Wave stands out as an investment due to the progress it has already made in commercial applications, while Rigetti offers an approach to building scalable quantum computing hardware that could be useful in a broader array of applications.

For investors who prefer to own companies with nearer-term commercial traction, D-Wave looks like the stronger fit. Rigetti is the higher-risk, higher-reward pick, since its success will depend on scaling superconducting systems. Both come with significant risks, but investors seeking names beyond IonQ may find their technologies and growth prospects worth considering.

Rick Orford has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Hewlett Packard Enterprise and IonQ. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Here’s what happened in crypto today

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto: Binance France is closing up shop in the country, as all accounts will be closed on Dec. 31. The US Treasury imposed financial sanctions on eight Canadian money-service companies it claims have done business with sanctioned ‌Iranian entities, and the Treasury also moved to cut MEXC off from the US financial system over alleged illicit transactions.

Binance France is closing up shop in the country. Customers have been notified by email during early November, according to media reports.

All accounts will be closed on Dec. 31, and any assets left on the exchange will be charged monthly fees starting on Nov. 29, Cryptoast, Crypto Briefing and other media reported on Saturday, citing emails sent to users by Binance.

On that date, accounts still maintaining a balance will pay a monthly maintenance fee of 0.025% of the balance, or a minimum of 10 USDC, a stablecoin tracking the US dollar.

To avoid fees, withdrawals must be completed by Nov. 29, the exchange said.

The US Treasury imposed financial sanctions on eight Canadian money-service companies it claims have done business with sanctioned ‌Iranian entities.

These companies facilitated the exchange of Iranian rials and other currencies for digital assets, enabling international transactions, a US Treasury Department statement said on Friday.

The sanctions, which serve to isolate the companies from the dollar-based financial system, are the latest in a series of Treasury moves against firms outside the US.

These businesses have facilitated transactions with sanctioned Iranian digital asset exchanges, as well as with digital asset addresses associated with Iran’s Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC), according to the Treasury.

The US Department of Treasury on Friday issued an order to cut cryptocurrency exchange MEXC Global off from the US’ financial system after accusing it of facilitating at least $16 billion in transactions linked to sanctions evasion, cybercrime and other illicit activity.

The Treasury’s Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) asserted that MEXC handled transactions involving sanctioned groups in Iran, Russia and North Korea between May 2021 and May 2026. This included around $1.5 billion in transactions linked to Russian sanctions evasion, according to the official press release.

The order effectively bans US financial institutions from processing money transfers involving MEXC and will take effect 30 days after it is published in the Federal Register.

“Financial institutions cannot have it both ways: Either you support the Iranian regime, or you have access to the U.S. financial system,” said Secretary of the Treasury Scott Bessent. “Treasury will continue to sever the Iranian regime’s economic lifelines and thwart its desperate attempts to circumvent U.S. sanctions using digital assets.”

The action is part of Operation Economic Outcast, a US Treasury campaign launched in August with aims to cut off financial entities that help Iran evade sanctions.

According to DefiLlama, the exchange held about $4.48 billion in total assets as of Friday. Cointelegraph contacted MEXC for comment but had not received a response by publication.

Morgan Stanley Says $300. Michael Burry Says Overvalued. Here's Who Is Right About SpaceX Stock.

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Experts are split on where SpaceX stock will go next.

Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +8.62%) stock remains exceptionally volatile. Shares currently trade about 17% lower than their first-day closing price back in early June. At various times this year, shares have rapidly shed more than one-third of their value, only to surge higher again by more than 50%.

The market seemingly can't make up its mind about SpaceX's valuation. Neither can the experts.

By one metric, most of Wall Street is in agreement regarding SpaceX stock. Out of 35 analysts covering the space stock, 29 have "buy" ratings, six have "hold" ratings, and just one has a "sell" rating. The consensus price target is around $250, indicating more than 86% upside potential.

When digging deeper, however, it becomes clear how divided Wall Street is. Some bullish analysts have price targets under $200. Others have price targets as high as $500. Somewhere in the middle is Morgan Stanley, with a price target of $300, implying more than 124% upside over the next 12 months.

Legendary investor Michael Burry -- a hedge fund manager who made billions for investors by shorting the U.S. housing market during the 2008-09 financial crisis -- disagrees.

"Nothing in that S-1 suggests it is worth $1 trillion let alone $2 trillion," Burry warned this summer, referring to SpaceX's IPO prospectus. Burry became even more cautious after SpaceX's valuation shot higher following its IPO. "At $2.8 TRILLION market cap, SpaceX, which is fundamentally a small space company, a niche telecom, a bedeviled social media company, and a Coreweave-light, has less than $20 billion in total revenue," he highlighted. "Any move up will be on hype and technicals."

With these concerns in mind, why does Morgan Stanley remain so bullish? Morgan Stanley's $300 price target comes down to a few key assumptions.

Nearly every analyst who covers SpaceX stock and is bullish focuses on one key operating segment: AI (artificial intelligence). According to SpaceX's own IPO prospectus, AI represents more than 90% of the company's total addressable market. Any bullish stance on SpaceX stock, therefore, can be assumed to include bullish assumptions regarding the company's AI segment.

Morgan Stanley, however, isn't just bullish on SpaceX's AI segment. Instead, analysts at the bank's investment arm are optimistic about how SpaceX's other segments will create synergies with its AI efforts. "We see the company as one of the few platforms that can link real estate in orbit, global connectivity, and compute capacity into one infrastructure stack," Morgan Stanley analysts explain.

In another note, the firm stressed that SpaceX's AI business is "the part of the SPCX story that we believe holds the greatest growth potential." The firm added that rockets and satellites will prove key to generating AI revenue. That's because SpaceX's Starship rocket will prove key to getting satellites into orbit cheaply at scale.

Those satellites, meanwhile, can be used not only by SpaceX's Starlink division to provide more connectivity services -- currently, a high-cash-flow contributor to SpaceX's bottom line -- but also to support orbital data centers. Orbital data centers could give SpaceX a cost and availability edge over other big tech competitors, though questions remain about the economic viability, as well as the technological and physical constraints of data centers in space.

This is the major difference between how Burry and Morgan Stanley view SpaceX stock. Morgan Stanley believes in synergies among SpaceX's business segments and forecasts significant AI growth driven by them. Burry, meanwhile, is more skeptical.

Notably, Morgan Stanley analysts affirm the potential for Burry's pessimistic valuation in their bear case. "Our $75 bear case assumes Starship slips to 2029 and AI monetization and deployment speed miss expectations, leaving Space and Connectivity at roughly 90% of valuation," Morgan Stanley analysts explain. "Ability to secure necessary capital is one of the greatest risks to our forecasts."

Morgan Stanley's bull case relies on SpaceX tapping huge amounts of new capital throughout the rest of the decade. Burry's argument essentially rests on one thing: The simultaneous success and synergistic potential of SpaceX's three segments combined with continued affordable access to capital for years on end is a difficult bet to make. It's hard to disagree with that sentiment. Which is why, like Burry, I am not long or short SpaceX stock but simply on the sidelines.

Ryan Vanzo has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Space Exploration Technologies. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

10 Pokémon That Are the Biggest Red Flags

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Aden Carter is a Contributor at DualShockers who has been covering games professionally since 2020. He specializes in lists and guides, with a focus on RPGs, horror games, survival horror, Soulslikes, cozy/sim games, and action-adventure titles. Before joining DualShockers, Aden contributed to GameRant, XDA, Gamepur, I

There are over 1,000 Pokémon now and they span nine generations of games. Over the years, there have been many Pokémon designs, with some of them being fantastic, and others being rather… questionable.

With that being said, everyone has a favorite Pokémon, but not all of them convey a positive or welcoming message. In fact, some of them are serious red flags to have as your favorite.

These Pokémon will make others cringe for obvious reasons, and possibly even question your taste. Sometimes it is about the Pokémon’s look, other times the Pokédex is to blame, and you can even blame society for others.

Charizard has been around since the beginning of the Pokémon series, and it quickly became a Pokémon that was beloved by everyone. Many of us were around to see Ash take in Charmander, and help it grow into a fiery beast.

However, there are many who consider Charizard to be a big red flag when choosing your favorite Pokémon. The reason for this is that you are far too basic if this is your choice.

Of course, there is a lot of context behind Charizard being a red flag. After all, it is the Pokémon that has received some of the most attention, with it getting multiple forms and promotions. It’s almost like Charizard is the mascot of Pokémon with how much attention it gets.

From the Eevee evolutions to Magikarp becoming something useful, here are the Pokémon in FireRed and LeafGreen that feel different after evolving.

The original generation of Pokémon was something that blew the minds of many kids. However, the magic that once created something that everyone loved ended up creating some rather poorly designed monsters in later generations.

One Pokémon design that felt uninspired is the Trubbish line, as it is nothing more than a bag of garbage that evolves into a pile of trash. Garbodor is nothing more than a trash pile that many associate with being smelly.

The internet ruined Garbodor by turning it into a meme where if you like the Pokémon, you are considered dirty. On the other hand, many associate this Pokémon as a red flag because choosing it feels like someone is purposefully trying to go against the grain by choosing a less universally loved Pokémon.

In past years, if you’d ask someone who their favorite Pokémon is, there was a large sect of the fan base that would say Typhlosion. He used to be cool, but everything changed when content was leaked that involved this once-innocent Pokémon.

As part of the leak in 2024, Typhlosion - specifically the Hisuian form - received some unsettling lore that involved folklore of it shapeshifting into a human and kidnapping a young girl. There’s more to it, but I don’t want to dive that deep.

Despite the leak never containing any canon information that made it into the games, and there being mistranslations, and other issues with it, Typhlosion has never really been able to escape the allegations, and so therefore, is still considered a red flag to many in the fan base.

When you bring together many people and give them the capability of being anonymous, as they can be on the internet, they have a habit of ruining everything you once loved. While there are multiple on this list, Lopunny is the first up.

Lopunny started off as Buneary - a rather adorable rabbit Pokémon - that gets an… interesting physique when it evolves into its final stage. Many saw this transformation, went online, and then proceeded to turn the Pokémon into a symbol for provocation.

Since then, it has been frowned upon whenever someone mentions that their favorite Pokémon is Lopunny, and the addition of Mega Lopunny did not help. Instead, it made things a bit worse.

Mr. Mime was an unsettling Pokémon when kids were introduced to it in the anime. Its human-like appearance mixed with a mime is something that is uncanny. However, it has only gotten worse over the years.

Digging through its Pokédex entries, you learn that it is capable of creating physical objects that are invisible. Along with that, if you interrupt its performance, it feels the need to slap you with its big flat hands.

This alone is weird, but you can add on the allegations that Mr. Mime is Ash’s stepfather in the anime. To cap it off, Mr. Mime got even more strange with the Detective Pikachu movie, further enhancing its uncanny and unsettling nature.

Did you choose Garchomp because you like the Pokémon, or did you choose it because it is a powerhouse and you are someone who gets overly competitive and sweaty during competition? Maybe it’s because you still have PTSD from Cynthia destroying you in Diamond and Pearl?

This is often what is associated with the love of Garchomp. There is no denying that Garchomp is an absolute beast of a Pokémon that can destroy others in competition, but that also makes many believe that those who like Garchomp have a far too competitive and aggressive mindset.

The other mindset people have is that Garchomp is associated with Cynthia from Diamond and Pearl. She dominated players in that era, and the idea is that those who like Garchomp haven’t gotten over that and want to inflict the same trauma on others.

There are Pokémon who have off Pokédex entries, with some of them being focused on some sort of trauma or horror story. Drifloon is one such Pokémon, with it being a ghost Pokémon who is known for kidnapping children.

The original Pokédex entries don’t make Drifloon out to be so bad, as the Dex says that it pulls on children to steal them away, but it ends up getting yanked around instead. However, this has gotten worse with time.

More recent entries specifically state that this Pokémon kidnaps children and whisks them off to the afterlife. Other entries talk about how it is specifically filled with souls and grows larger each time it steals one away. Let’s just leave this Pokémon out of our favorites list.

Pokemon Red/Blue's Lavender Town remains one of the eeriest creations in video gaming.

The internet has a way of taking any Pokémon that has slightly humanoid features and turning them into your worst nightmare. Similar to Lopunny before it, Gothitelle fell victim to those who seek to ruin things for other people.

Again, this is another Pokémon evolution line that starts off very innocent, with Gothita being a rather cute Pokémon that is very innocent in appearance. Unfortunately, that changes when it reaches its final evolution.

There is nothing innately wrong about liking this evolution line, but it definitely calls your taste into question, and many will immediately see the red flag fly, and it is completely understandable.

I am not sure that this even needs to be said because, of all the Pokémon with a humanoid appearance, Gardevoir has been treated the worst. Originally introduced in generation 3, the entire Raltz evolution line was loved, but then things went south very quickly.

Gardevoir is a Pokémon that resembles a woman in a flowing dress. Its body consists of said dress, which is more or less a flowing gown, and it has a hairstyle that appears to be done for a pageant or evening event.

However, this is enough to get the internet riled up, and Gardevoir was the first of the Pokémon of this style to be twisted by those who just seek to ruin things for other people. Now, this Pokémon is considered one of the biggest red flags of the franchise.

Eevee was, and still is, one of the most popular Pokémon there is, with many finding the little fox-like creature to be adorable. When it was introduced, it was beloved, and so were its original three evolutions; Flareon, Jolteon, and Vaporeon.

Unfortunately for Vaporeon, it became the fascination of one of the most perverse corners of the Pokémon fan base. So much so that it was treated far worse than Lopunny, Gothitelle, and Gardivoire combined.

The information about this Pokémon soon became a meme that spread like wildfire, leading to many victims being caught in the smoke. Now, many are ashamed to even talk about Vaporeon in a positive light, as it looks bad. What was once one of the most famous Pokémon of a generation has now become the biggest red flag in the fandom.

While most pokemon are friendly and nice, some are much more mischievous and ready to cause harm. Here are Pokemon ready to rob you in broad daylight

Streaming vs. downloading: Which uses more battery and data?

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Video and sound quality have an impact on battery and data usage.

Although some people are pushing back, a lot of us are dependent on streaming services for our movies and music. There's an obvious incentive — you get a massive library for a (relatively) cheap monthly fee and the ability to access that media from virtually any device with an internet connection. Conversely, owned downloads and physical media can be pricey and not terribly portable.

Many streaming services do offer a middle ground of sorts, letting you cache songs, videos or podcasts so you can play them with or without an internet connection. But let's say you're most concerned with efficiency and not the soundtrack to a remote camping expedition. Does it still make more sense to download in advance, or should you stream whenever possible?

The quick answer is that downloads (of all stripes) are usually the most efficient option when it comes to battery and usage. But there may be times when streaming is preferable, or possibly the only option.

For anything you enjoy on a regular basis, downloads win. While there's going to be an initial (and possibly intense) surge of battery and bandwidth consumption, that's vastly outweighed by the non-existent effort for subsequent plays. You should always cache over Wi-Fi, incidentally, since it's usually faster, and a way to avoid bumping up against the data caps that many cellular providers continue to impose on their plans.

It's only when you're talking about one-time plays that streaming is in any way efficient. If you hit Stop or Pause partway through a video or playlist with no intent of coming back, you may consume less power and data. We say "may" because all streaming apps perform a base amount of caching, whether or not you deliberately save anything. Depending on where you halt during playback, your device may already have a complete video or playlist in memory, which is quickly going to be wasted.

Really, the only reason to avoid downloading is storage space. Just a few cached playlists in a music app can consume several gigabytes, speaking from personal experience, and that's without factoring lossless options into the equation. In app settings, Spotify notes that its lossless downloads can consume up to 1 GB per hour of playback — which will probably make downloading your entire library impractical, if not impossible.

Perhaps the easiest way to rein things in is by adjusting media quality. Returning to the Spotify example, even scaling back from lossless quality to "Very high" (320 kbps) drops data consumption from 1 GB per hour to just 0.14 GB, according to the music streaming site. Lower data use naturally means reduced battery drain, since caching is faster. Similar principles apply to video apps, although they tend to manage quality via resolution options instead of bitrate. All else being equal, a 480p or 720p version of a video has a far smaller footprint than 1080p. Depending on your app and device, 4K downloads may not even be an option.

You should only scale things back so far. While lossless and 320 kbps are virtually indistinguishable in audio, you'll notice a lack of clarity by the time you drop to 160 kbps. Similarly, although you're probably not going to spot the difference between 1080p and 4K on something as small as a phone or tablet, 720p is below the sharpness of most displays.

There may be smarter ways of reducing battery and data use, such as lowering screen brightness, and being more selective over what you consume in general. Your life doesn't always need a soundtrack or video wallpaper. And if you limit the number of apps, podcasts and playlists on your device, there's less to update in the background.

Prediction: This Glorious Growth Stock Is Going to Soar in 2027

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This stock has already surged by more than 80% from its 52-week low, and more upside might be ahead.

Duolingo (DUOL +2.17%) operates the world's largest digital language education platform. Its mobile-first approach places fun, highly interactive lessons at the fingertips of practically anybody with a smartphone, and making this style of education accessible has been the secret to its long-term success.

Duolingo plummeted to a 52-week low of $90.03 in April, which represented a whopping 83% decline from last year's record high. Investors were concerned about the consequences of management's new strategy to focus on user growth instead of monetization, and they were also worried that artificial intelligence (AI) could disrupt its business.

However, Duolingo has put those concerns to rest by posting strong operating results, particularly in the recent third quarter (ended Sept. 30). Its stock has now soared by 83% from its 52-week low (as of the market close on Nov. 4), and here's why I predict the recovery will continue in 2027.

Historically, Duolingo has spent very little money on marketing because its widespread, organic social media presence has been enough to attract new users. However, the company grew its marketing spending by 20% during the third quarter, so management is now leaning into paid channels to accomplish its goals.

Moreover, Duolingo continues to make product changes geared toward attracting and retaining free users. For example, the proportion of voice-based speaking exercises on the platform have doubled since the start of 2026. These are typically more popular than tap-based or written exercises because they help users learn more quickly.

Duolingo generated $1.18 billion in revenue over the last four quarters, placing its stock at a price-to-sales (P/S) ratio of 6.8. That is less than half its average of 14.9 since going public in 2021. Moreover, Wall Street's average estimate (provided by Yahoo! Finance) suggests Duolingo could grow its revenue to $1.37 billion in 2027, placing its stock at a forward P/S ratio of 5.5.

That means even if the stock doubles over the next 12 months, its P/S ratio will still be below its long-term average. That leaves plenty of room for upside next year.

Duolingo stock peaked at $540 last year, and I think it could return to that level in the future, so investors who are willing to hold the stock for a long-term period of three years or more could earn significant returns.

Anthony Di Pizio has positions in Duolingo. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Duolingo. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Missing teenager rescued after reaching out to another player in Among Us

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"Our priority in this case was the safe return of the minor victim."

A teenager who went missing in Utah more than 17 months ago has been rescued from a home in Oregon after she got in touch with another young player in Among Us.

According to a federal criminal complaint reported by Newsweek and KUTV (via IGN), the girl, who was 15 when she disappeared from Garden City, Utah, on 29th May 2025, began chatting with another teenager in Among Us on or around 15th October this year.

The pair later moved their conversation to a different social media app, where she told them who she was and that she was being held against her will in Oregon. She had been given a phone with no cellular data, which meant she could only get online via wi-fi.

The other teen told their parent, who contacted the FBI on 1st November. Agents listened in on a video call between the parent and the girl, and she was recovered from the house later that day. She is now 17.

Adam Hunter Harmel, 39, of Huntington, Oregon, was arrested during a traffic stop.

According to the US Attorney's Office for the District of Utah, he allegedly met the girl online in May 2025, then drove to Utah to pick her up and took her back to his home across state lines. He was charged on 2nd November with transportation with intent to engage in criminal sexual activity, and made his first court appearance in Boise, Idaho, on 4th November.

"Our priority in this case was the safe return of the minor victim," said US Attorney Melissa Holyoak. "Those who allegedly exploit and prey upon the most vulnerable members of our communities will be relentlessly investigated and held accountable."

Utah's US Attorney's Office stresses that a complaint is merely an allegation, and that all defendants are presumed innocent until proven guilty in a court of law.

The Gambit-Related Mistake Channing Tatum Nearly Made: ‘It Would’ve Just Been A Catastrophe’

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

After Patrick Stewart’s Death, Anthony Mackie’s Take On Working With Him On Doomsday Hit Me Hard

I’m Impressed That Inde Navarrette Used An Obsession Reference To Dodge An X-Men Question

A 'New' Blade Isn't Happening, But I Have An Idea To Get The Character Into The MCU

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

2024’s Deadpool & Wolverine was the realization of a dream for Channing Tatum, as he was finally able to debut his big screen character Gambit. Years before that, Tatum had been eager to play the fan-favorite X-Man and was to do so in a solo film. That flick ultimately didn’t pan out, but a lot of work was put in beforehand. Tatum recently recalled trying to get that comic book movie off the ground and, while doing so, he revealed one major mistake he could've made amid development.

Tatum was first linked to the role of cajun card thrower Remy LeBeau around 2014, at which point it was reported that he’d debut in 2016’s X-Men: Apocalypse before leading his own movie. He even promoted the film at Comic-Con in 2014. During a recent interview with ScreenRant, Tatum promoted Dunkin’ Donuts’ promotional campaign for the upcoming Avengers: Doomsday, in which he’ll reprise Remy. The Magic Mike alum was asked if a solo film centered around his character was still a possibility, and he didn’t strike down the idea.

However, if that X-Men spinoff were to happen, something Tatum wouldn’t be bullish about is directing himself, and that was something he aimed to do when he was developing the film years ago.

As the Magic Mike alum explained, he and his producing partner, Reid Carolin, were motivated to make the movie by “frustration and hubris,” and they went through quite a few directors:

We had three different directors that were signed up to do it, and the first one, the studio, Fox, they had a fallout, so that one didn't work out. The second one was just in development for so long with somebody that I was just like, ‘We got to move to making this thing.’ So we separated ourselves, and then the third one almost got going with Gore Verbinski, and then creatively all three of us, the studio, him and me, we started in the same place, and then as it developed, we just all grew apart.

Following all that, Tatum and Carolin apparently approached 20th Century Fox about commissioning a script “that the studio wanted to make, and that we wanted to make.” The hope was for that script to attract a filmmaker who would want to direct it as it was. However, after seeing the screenplay, the pair loved the script, and that passion fueled the idea that one of them helm the flick. That eagerness only grew after Shawn Levy – who, ironically, directed D&W years later – praised their script and expressed interest in helming Gambit.

Channing Tatum is now grateful to have not directed the movie, as it’s his belief that neither he nor Reid Carolin were prepared to direct a film of that scale. The Jump Street star even went so far as to say that the production “would've failed miserably” had he or Carolin helmed it. He also said this:

Now that I know exactly what directing is and how complicated it is and how these movies specifically are so hard, you really need to know what you're doing. It can't be your first movie. You've got to really get a lot of different facets under your belt and have a lot of experience because making one of these movies is nearly impossible. Some people really have the formula down and really know how to do it, and some others struggle. It would've just been a catastrophe on a level that I would never have recovered from.

I can certainly understand the concerns, especially considering how big a superhero movie can be. Tatum may have been “traumatized” by the movie not panning out, but it sounds like he learned a valuable lesson from the experience. Plus, it’s worth mentioning that Tatum has since made his directorial debut with the critically well received road dramedy Dog, which hit theaters in 2022.

Tatum is also blessed in that he’s still in the position to play Gambit, who delighted audiences (and Ryan Reynolds) with his sick moves and thick cajun accent. Based on the Dunkin’ ad, Remy’s cadence may be a bit more tame in Doomsday. I’m not sure if Tatum’s version of LeBeau will appear beyond the team-up film, especially due to the upcoming X-Men reboot. But, if the door of opportunity swings open, Tatum will walk through it, even if he doesn’t seem keen on directing.

Avengers: Doomsday opens in theaters on December 18 amid the 2026 movie schedule. Fans can also check out Channing Tatum’s performance as Gambit in Deadpool & Wolverine by streaming the film with a Disney+ subscription.

Hundreds arrested after Microsoft data center protest turns violent

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The people were protesting against the use of fossil fuel generators to power data centers.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Amsterdam police have arrested 150 to 200 protesters at a Microsoft data center site on suspicion of trespassing. According to a Facebook post by the authorities [machine translated], tools designed for breaking into the site were seized from the protesters, who were then allowed on their way to protest peacefully. However, it appears that some members of the group decided to enter the construction site “with violence” anyway, forcing the police to intervene and leading to their arrest. There were also some allegedly stolen items from the construction site found among the belongings of some of the protesters, plus damage and vandalism done to the data center site.

This protest is a continuation of the action that started last Friday, where protesters breached the Microsoft data center, which was being owned by Pure Data Centers. It is also the first of many organized by the “Stop Dirty Data Centers” coalition across Europe, with actions planned for Amsterdam, Dublin, Warsaw, Denmark, and several other cities across the continent. The Dutch publication NOS [machine translated] identified the exact group at the site today as Fossielvrij, who are against the use of fossil fuels in data centers such as the jet engines that Elon Musk installed at xAI’s Mississippi data center.

“Gas prices are going through the roof, and new houses are being postponed because of the overcrowded power grid. Yet tech billionaires like Elon Musk and Jeff Bezos are building energy-guzzling, hideous data centers,” the group said on its website. “We can take back the power of Big Tech by pressing the pause button now. Give us the opportunity to democratically decide what we do and do not use AI for, so that it is not at the expense of our living environment and everything that makes our lives beautiful. Then we look at how many more data centers are needed.”

The protest comes amid the AI data center build-out, which has been facing increased opposition across the United States. A Google data center site in Finland is also in trouble after it cleared out around 1,300 acres worth of forest and trees without an environmental impact assessment, displacing wildlife, and leading to a criminal probe on Tuike Finland Oy, the Google subsidiary in charge of the site.

Fossielvrij’s action was supposed to be peaceful, but it appears that another group joined the demonstration and broke through the fences around the site, leading to the police confrontation. The suspects were arrested for domestic violence and then taken to a cell complex, where it will be decided if they will be detained longer. The police also said that “All suspects will be heard afterward.”

Meet Gayane Umerova, the Woman Putting Uzbekistan’s Arts and Culture Scene on the Map

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’s been a landmark year for Uzbekistan. The country held its inaugural biennial in the ancient city of Bukhara last fall; 10 Soviet modernist buildings in its captial, Tashkent, were named UNESCO World Heritage Sites over the summer, making them the youngest structures in the world to receive that protected status; and its first permanent contemporary art museum, the Centre for Contemporary Arts Tashkent (CCA Tashkent), opened in September.

Upon meeting Gayane Umerova—the woman behind Uzbekistan’s cultural awakening—one of my first questions is whether she was surprised by the biennial’s success.

“My expectations are always very high, so it didn’t really surprise me. It was exactly what I envisioned,” Umerova says. What did surprise her: that out of the 1.8 million visitors to the Bukhara Biennial, half were domestic. “That’s a big shift in society. For someone to travel from Kokand to Bukhara [some 460 miles away], that would never really happen unless you’re visiting relatives. Now people see Bukhara not only as a historical city, but also as somewhere exciting to discover.”

Her official titles include Head of the Department of Creative Economy and Tourism of the Administration of the President of the Republic of Uzbekistan, as well as Chairperson of the Uzbekistan Art and Culture Development Foundation (ACDF), an organization she founded in 2017 to support the country’s visual artists, writers, theatermakers, musicians, and choreographers. (We’re meeting in her office at the ACDF headquarters in Tashkent, a space tastefully decorated with sleek wooden furniture made by Levantine designer Nada Debs in collaboration with Uzbek artisans—another one of Umerova’s ideas.) She additionally serves as Chairperson of the National Commission of Uzbekistan on UNESCO Affairs under the Cabinet of Ministers, was awarded France’s Order of Arts and Literature in April 2025, and is a LACMA Global Ambassador. It’s no wonder that when I ask how she spends her free time, she retorts that she has none—though she does relish family time with her husband and three daughters.

“Because Uzbekistan is not a country where you have a lot of private collectors building museums or architectural sites, everything has to start with the state support and then rise within society,” Umerova explains, crediting the flurry of activity in Tashkent—like that in Doha or Riyadh—to the attention and investment of its leadership, namely Uzbekistan’s president, Shavkat Mirziyoyev.

Uzbekistan’s large (and growing) subset of young people also makes the country ripe for a cultural renaissance. As of July 2026, more than half of Uzbekistan’s 39 million people are under 30. “You see it often in countries like Uzbekistan or Saudi Arabia, where there is a young population that they drive you,” Umerova says. “You meet people and see they’re creating things from nothing, and that’s really what motivated me to be in the art world.”

Much of Umerova’s desire to strengthen her country’s cultural ecosystem stems from her own childhood in Tashkent. With an aunt who was an artist and grandparents who collected antiquities, she grew up surrounded by the arts, though her first dream was to become an architect. Her father tried to steer her elsewhere, worried that she would be crowded out of such a male-dominated field: “He didn’t want me to end up behind the scenes of these great projects, like a secretary at a big firm.” Nevertheless, Umerova’s passion for architecture has come full circle, from her UNESCO advocacy to her partnerships with firms like the Paris-based Studio KO—which designed the CCA Tashkent—and Japanese starchitect Tadao Ando, who is currently at work on the new National Museum of Uzbekistan, scheduled to open in the capital in 2028.

Umerova received much of her education in the United Kingdom, discovering in Europe what she hoped to create at home. “I’d never seen museums like the Pompidou, which was so extravagant to me at the time,” she recalls. “I was inspired not just by the arts but the buildings that house them. I felt like I would love to do something like that in my own country.” Back in Tashkent, she served as a senior curator at the Art Gallery of Uzbekistan from 2008 to 2017, the year she started the ACDF.

The majority of Umerova’s roughly 50-person team at the ACDF has lived or studied abroad. “I think we have the stigma of not hearing or seeing our country on the map, but now the younger population really wants to own that they’re from this country,” she says. She believes it has become a “collective ambition” to strengthen the country’s arts-education infrastructure from a young age. (In service of that goal, the ACDF was behind the renovation of the Nationwide Children’s Library, which has operated out of a 19th-century mansion in Tashkent since 1965.)

This demanded, in part, a reframing of what even constituted art. “In Uzbekistan, there are a lot of artisans, but they just see the traditional way of getting their work to people, for example, through fairs. They don’t really see themselves as artists. They only see themselves as makers.” She traces that sensibility back to the fall of the Soviet Union, when, “for all Central Asian countries, it was about self-identity,” and “everything which was contemporary or modern was seen as foreign.”

While Umerova acknowledges the value of promoting Uzbek crafts, from woodcarving to papermaking, she sees no reason why contemporary art and design can’t also thrive. In 2022, the ACDF formed a partnership with the Michelangelo Foundation, marking the first time a Central Asian country joined its Homo Faber Guide, a curated digital platform spotlighting master artisans and ateliers around the globe. Uzbekistan also sent its first representatives to the Venice Architecture Biennale and the Venice Art Biennale in 2021 and 2022, respectively.

A testament to Uzbekistan’s success in Venice is London-based Uzbek artist Aziza Kadyri, who combines suzani, traditional hand-embroidered textiles, with AI. She participated in the Uzbekistan National Pavilion at the 2024 Venice Art Biennale, and last year she was commissioned for the Bukhara Biennial. (Visitors to the Victoria & Albert Museum in London can see a site-specific installation in which Kadyri responds to the museum’s historic suzani collection until January 10, 2027.)

Venice’s Biennale model has long inspired Umerova. “It’s like a living city with art,” she says, emphasizing the convenience of having most sites within walking distance of each other. With restoration at the heart of the Bukhara Biennial (the Historic Centre of Bukhara was added to the World Heritage List in 1993), Umerova conceived a master plan to ensure that the art and the impending tourist boom would not harm the city, the most complete medieval city in Central Asia. The inaugural edition saw local artists, such as ceramicist Abdulvahid Bukhoriy and metal engraver Jurabek Sidikov, transform a prayer room in a restored historic madrasa (a center of Islamic education), while participating international artists ranged from Egyptian multidisciplinary artist Wael Shawky to British sculptor Antony Gormley.

Of course, Uzbeks can now get their contemporary art fix year-round at the CCA Tashkent. Curated by Sara Raza, who specializes in postcolonial and post-Soviet art, the debut exhibition, “Hikmah”—Uzbek for “wisdom”—brings together works by artists near and far. The painstaking restoration of the building, originally constructed in 1912 as a diesel station, has also made a powerful statement about the potential of adaptive reuse.

Exterior of the Centre for Contemporary Arts Tashkent (CCA Tashkent).

The CCA Tashkent, Uzbekistan’s first permanent contemporary art museum, is housed in a former diesel station.

Conscious that modern buildings are often considered less precious than ancient sites—and therefore more vulnerable to being torn down—Umerova spent five years researching and applying for Tashkent’s Soviet modernist buildings to be granted UNESCO status. She also advocated for about 160 mosaic panels adorning residential and public buildings in Tashkent and other areas to be added to the National List of Real Estate Properties of Tangible Cultural Heritage in 2024. After an earthquake destroyed much of Tashkent in 1966, these mosaic designs were embedded into new constructions, becoming a distinctive part of Uzbekistan’s Soviet-era architectural landscape. Under this heritage status, the mosaics are protected from being dismantled, painted over, or covered with advertising.

Several more museums are underway in Uzbekistan, most notably the new National Museum, Tadao Ando’s first major project in Central Asia. The architect tells Vogue via email that he hopes the 40,000-square-meter institution “will become a spiritual anchor for its people and serve as a vibrant cultural center.” Its collection, currently housed in the State Museum of Arts (which will become a state museum of architecture—fitting, as it was one of the 10 buildings to earn UNESCO protection this year), is especially important to Umerova, as she often visited it with her parents and on school trips. The ACDF is also working on the Jadids’ Legacy Museum in Bukhara and the Silk Road Museum in Samarkand. They are projected to open in 2027 and 2029–30, respectively.

A rendering of the National Museum of Uzbekistan.

Umerova’s efforts have also reached American institutions. New York’s Solomon R. Guggenheim Museum loaned a monumental aerial sculpture by Nadia Kaabi-Linke, a Tunisian-Ukrainian artist based in Berlin, for the CCA Tashkent’s inaugural exhibition; and Umerova teases a major collaboration with the Smithsonian in 2029 that will have been 10 years in the making.

“Gayane Umerova is leading the cultural strategy for Uzbekistan with an uncommon combination of inspiring vision, executive follow-through, and a fine sense of what art means for human thriving and cohesion as well as social and economic development,” Dr. Mariët Westermann, director and CEO of the Solomon R. Guggenheim Foundation, told Vogue via email of Umerova’s “broad-spectrum” approach.

Umerova’s hope is that Uzbekistan will not just be a place more travelers want to visit, but one that they keep coming back to. “We want people to see our country on repeat,” she says. “Not just once in a lifetime.”

Aurbis converts Bethesda's fantasy adventure into a shared multiplayer world, with all the systems that entails.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

PC gaming isn't short of multiplayer fantasy adventures these days, but there is still something alluring about the idea of playing Skyrim with pals. Some mods have facilitated small-scale Skyrim multiplayer in the past, like the cooperative mod Skyrim Together. But Aurbis goes several step further, converting Bethesda's RPG into a persistent online roleplaying server—essentially a Skyrim MMO in all but name.

According to its ModDB page, Aurbis transforms Skyrim's solo adventure into "a persistent world where player actions create lasting consequences", snythesising Skyrim's existing experience with a bunch of multiplayer and MMO-like systems. It's designed with roleplaying in mind, though the mod's creators stress that RP is not mandatory, and players can focus on other elements like cooperative dungeon runs or PvP play if they so choose.

The list of changes and additions to Aurbis is impressive, running from character creation outward. The retooled character builder is designed to let avatars "develop over time instead of becoming powerful immediately", though it also lets players start with vampirism or lycanthropy for roleplaying purposes.

Those roles are heavily defined by the player's choice of Birthsign, which affects how efficiently skills develop. Crafting professions are supported by a player-driven economy that factors in bank accounts, property ownership and player-created quests, while activities like combat are designed to be dangerous and reward "preparation, equipment, specialisation and teamwork."

Alongside these changes are entirely new, multiplayer specific systems. Players can form their own groups called Cohorts, designed to act as a "strong foundation for MMO-style social gameplay". This ranges from establishing parties and guilds to aligning yourself with Skyrim's existing factions, combining "familiar Skyrim content with custom multiplayer mechanics".

The list doesn't end there, either. There are more nuanced survival systems, complex horse management, MMO style death penalties, and much more. There's even a fully-fledged language and literacy system whereby players "do not automatically understand every conversation or know every location", so mastering language and literacy can provide a genuine roleplaying advantage.

It sounds wild, and this only represents an early access release. If you fancy investigating Aurbis, all you need is a legitimate copy of Skyrim that's downgraded to version 1.6.1170. The beta is invite-only, but a session key is listed on the ModDB page that should grant you access, while Aurbis has a custom launcher that downloads and maintains all required mods.

Following Assassin's Creed Shadows' and Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced's success, Ubisoft is apparently struggling to strike while the iron's hot. Its next mainline game in the series - codenamed Assassin's Creed Hexe - has reportedly been delayed internally, while the publisher works on two other AC games.

AC Hexe was actually announced alongside Shadows more than four years ago, though we haven't had a concrete look at the supposedly witchy, spooky open-world game since. All we knew for sure is that Hexe would launch sometime after Shadows, and here we are now.

Insider Gaming now reports that Assassin's Creed Hexe ran into further development road bumps and has since been internally delayed by another year, citing anonymous sources in the know. According to the report, the likelier release date for Hexe could be sometime in 2028.

AC Hexe hasn't had the smoothest journey to launch so far, with or without another internal delay. The game's original creative director Clint Hocking left the company earlier this year, with Black Flag and Origins' lead developer Jean Guesdon taking over for him. Two months later, game director Benoit Richer also departed.

Still, Ubisoft's purportedly cooking up more than one dish in its historical and historically stealthy-stabby series. Well connected YouTuber j0nathan recently claimed there are two other codenamed AC games in development - Assassin's Creed Mars and Assassin's Creed Pocket.

AC Mars supposedly takes the series to Ancient Rome for the first time, perhaps tying into the upcoming Netflix show in some way. Assassin's Creed Brotherhood did take place in Rome during the Italian Renaissance era, so there's probably fun ways for the lore to connect if Ubisoft did ever parkour down this route. On the other hand, Pocket is apparently set in Jerusalem and is said to be a mobile game, hence the name. As always, take any rumors with a handful of salt until Ubisoft confirms plans for itself.

So, there's lots of Assassin's Creed flying about the place. To keep up with everything going on, check out every upcoming Assassin's Creed game currently in development.

A celebration of peak Joe Cole: ‘A special player, full of fantasy’

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Every English football fan of a certain age should have a special place in their hearts for Joe Cole. Once upon a time, he was the nation’s greatest hope.

No matter how many people warn against heaping too much pressure on young players’ shoulders, in England we just can’t help it. The country is constantly looking for a new hero.

Even in the digital age of constant hyperbole, however, few players have had to handle the intense fever which surrounded Joe Cole’s embryonic days as a burgeoning professional footballer.

At the age of 15, Cole was earning rave reviews in the national press thanks to his performances for England schoolboys, attracting interest from the likes of Arsenal and Manchester United.

By 17, he was being invited by Kevin Keegan to train with the senior England squad after inspiring West Ham to a 9-0 thrashing of Coventry City over two legs in the final of the FA Youth Cup.

And just to temper expectations, he was one of the first teenagers to be dubbed ‘The Next Gazza‘.

When Cole was paraded at Upton Park during half-time of a league match against Chelsea to sign his first pro contract, the stadium announcer declared: “You can tell your grandchildren you were here when Joe Cole signed.”

Cole’s career since the end of the noughties created the perception that he failed to live up to his immense potential, but that’s unfair. If not necessarily ever the best English footballer, for a period between 2004-09, Cole was undoubtedly one of the most fun. That alone makes him worthy of celebration.

One of the things which always made Cole so alluring is that he was so obviously mad about football. He always seemed most at home on the pitch, with the ball at his feet and a defender stood in his way, primed to be embarrassed.

By the time he had broken into West Ham’s first team, Cole was already asking Harry Redknapp if he could avoid giving interviews. The boy from Camden only ever had one thing on his mind: football.

He went on to make 150 appearances for the Hammers and became renowned for his flair and trickery. Those sort of players are few and far between in England, and Cole was a welcome breath of fresh air; a footballer seemingly born to entertain.

As a child he had paid particularly close attention to Italian and African football, making a note of the latest piece of skill and honing it himself at the next available opportunity.

Rather than be overawed by the seriousness of top-flight football, Cole carried the same attitude against his senior pros, with the advice of his coach at Camden and Islington Boys, Terry Howard, ringing in his ears: “Do what you like from now on, son.”

“I’ve been coaching for 20 years and have never seen such a combination of flair, pace and commitment,” Howard said of his first impressions of the youngster in an interview with The Independent in 1999. “He was also the most modest.”

Come 2002-03, and still at the tender age of 21, Cole was made West Ham captain by Glenn Roeder and flourished with the added responsibility.

West Ham may have been unable to avoid relegation, but Cole’s valiant displays in midfield earned him the club’s Player of the Year award and a £6.6million move to Chelsea, where he was labelled the “new Gianfranco Zola” by Claudio Ranieri. The unrelenting expectation continued to increase.

However, handed the No.10 shirt, his debut season with the Blues was somewhat underwhelming. While he made 50 appearances in all competitions, exactly half of those were from the bench, and he did not score in the league until April as he struggled to find a suitable role in the team.

It would be a theme which continued throughout his career. Cole played at a time when English football transitioned from 4-4-2 to 4-3-3, neither formation particularly allowing for the indulgence of a free-spirited No.10.

It was the arrival of Jose Mourinho at Chelsea which prompted the best football of Cole’s career. If the game was unwilling to adapt to his talents, he could at least adapt himself under the guidance of one of the most tactically astute managers in modern football.

Two things helped Cole rediscover his verve at Chelsea.

First, John Terry, who Cole has described as “like an older brother” at the time, asked one of the video analysts to pull together a compilation of all the midfielder’s highlights to remind him of his unique ability.

But the main turning point came in October 2004. Cole walked off the pitch at Stamford Bridge having scored an excellent winner against Liverpool, only to be publicly criticised by Mourinho for a lack of defensive application.

“He has two faces – one beautiful and one I don’t like,” Mourinho said. “He must keep one and change the other one.”

﻿

Rather than take the criticism to heart, Cole had the belief in his own game to go out and prove his manager wrong. After all, even though he was still only 22, it was all part and parcel of being Joe Cole.

“We are on the same page,” he said. “He wants Chelsea to win games and I want Chelsea to win games. He wants Joe Cole to be a better player than he already is – and so do I. I have always taken criticism well and it wasn’t massive criticism in my eyes.

“We both want to make me a better player for Chelsea and if that is the end result then that is all that matters. It hurt when people said I was showing off. All I like to do is put a smile on fans’ faces as part of the side.

“What people must appreciate is I have had this attention since I was 16 at West Ham. The expectancy was enormous then and hasn’t changed.”

From that moment on, Cole established himself as one of Mourinho’s go-to men on the wing, starting the League Cup final victory over Liverpool and both legs of the Champions League semi-final defeat to the same opponents.

He ended the season with nine goals in 46 appearances, with only 12 of those outings coming from the bench. Most importantly, he also had a Premier League winner’s medal.

The following season continued in much the same manner. With the fully-earned trust of Mourinho, Cole went one better in terms of both appearances and goals, despite the added competition from Shaun Wright-Phillips alongside Damien Duff and Arjen Robben.

And at the end of that 2005-06 campaign, Cole produced one of his finest moments in a blue shirt.

With Manchester United arriving at Stamford Bridge in April, Chelsea had the chance to clinch the title against their nearest rivals. Fittingly, it was Cole who produced the piece of skill worthy of winning a championship.

Receiving the ball with his back to goal and three United defenders around him just outside the box, Cole somehow left all three for dead with the deception of the world’s greatest conman before firing the ball home with an emphatic finish.

It's Matchday!Manchester United vs Chelsea FCOld Trafford5.30pm (UK Time)#COYB 💙 pic.twitter.com/DYJ0IfjjrZ

— ChelseaFC News & Vids (@CFCclips) December 28, 2015

In one moment Cole showed both the absurd talent which first attracted him so much hype at West Ham and the refinement of his game at Chelsea which made him the country’s best attacking midfielder.

Later that summer, Cole also produced the highlight of his England career with his stunning, dipping volley against Sweden at the World Cup.

It was another example of him attempting something most other players wouldn’t even dream of.

“Joe Cole has always been a special player, full of fantasy,” Sven-Goran Eriksson had commented four years previous. Finally, he was proving it on a regular basis.

#OnThisDay in 2006, Joe Cole scored THAT volley from 35 yards out against Sweden. pic.twitter.com/gDHF4keQDd

— Planet Football (@planetfutebol) June 20, 2020

In the following three seasons Cole cemented his status as one of English football’s favourite sons, collecting the FA Cup twice, another League Cup and coming a John Terry slip away from winning the Champions League.

But in January 2009, Cole ruptured his anterior cruciate ligament playing in an FA Cup replay at Southend, and his career would never be the same.

Upon his return, nine months later, he never fully established himself in what was then Carlo Ancelotti’s side before his contract expired in the summer.

“Ever since I did my knee at Chelsea, it was an uphill struggle,” he told the Daily Mail in 2017.

Cole’s last appearance for Chelsea came in the FA Cup final victory over Portsmouth which secured the club’s first ever Double, and there were still enough flashes of inspiration – namely his back-heeled opener in a win at Old Trafford – to convince Fabio Capello to name him in England’s squad for the 2010 World Cup.

Joe Cole v Manchester United in PL 2009/10

— CFC SAUDI FANS ™ (@CFCSAUDIFANS) August 6, 2019

It speaks volumes about how he was regarded that Terry tried to lead a mutiny against the manager to ensure Cole was selected after a dismal display against Algeria devoid of any spark.

“Listen, Joe is one of the best players in our country,” he said. “He has come back from an injury and showed great form for Chelsea. It still amazes me how the club have come to the conclusion of letting Joe go.”

But Cole was only granted two substitute appearances, and his club career similarly failed to take off thereafter.

A move to Liverpool never recovered after a red card on his debut and a missed penalty in his second appearance.

Though he did impress at Lille alongside Dimitri Payet and Eden Hazard – we’re not ashamed to admit that resembles something close to our wet dream – a return to West Ham and short spell at Aston Villa also fell flat.

To his credit, Cole continued to battle against his body, and dropped down two divisions to rekindle his love for the game at Coventry City before heading west for an Indian Summer with the Tampa Bay Rowdies.

While many are tempted to label Cole a waste of potential, that would be unfair. He is one of only 11 Englishmen to have won three Premier League titles, three FA Cups and played in three World Cups.

“Once you stop learning, there’s no point playing,” he told The Guardian in 2011. “You’d have mastered it. And no one’s mastered football yet.”

Joe Cole shouldn’t be used as a warning to young footballers, he should be used as an example.

Who is King Ghidorah? The cosmic connections of Godzilla Minus Zero's foe

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The three-headed Kaiju's origins aren't just space-based; one of the largest structures in the universe shares its name.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Godzilla goes toe-to-toe with long-time foe King Ghidorah in Godzilla Minus Zero, which opened in theaters on Friday (Nov. 6). Though the three-headed golden Kaiju might seem like a natural native of Monster Island, King Ghidorah actually emerged from the depths of space.

However, it isn't just the origins of King Ghidorah — also known as Monster Zero in the Godzilla movie Invasion of Astro-Monster (1965) — that are this beast's only cosmic connection. One of the largest structures in the universe, the King Ghidorah Supercluster, was named in honor of Godzilla's primary antagonist.

King Ghidorah is a three-headed, twin-tailed golden dragon from the stars, first introduced in the fifth Godzilla movie, Ghidorah, the Three-Headed Monster (1964).

In this first appearance, part of the Shōwa era (1964–1975) of Godzilla history, Ghidorah ravaged Venus, wiping out its population 5,000 years before arriving on Earth encased within a highly magnetic meteorite. Ghidorah was bested this first time by Godzilla, Rodan, and Mothra.

Undeterred, Ghidorah returned for a rematch just a year later in Invasion of Astro Monster (1965), after being discovered supposedly terrorizing the inhabitants of Planet X, a world found beyond Jupiter. In this movie, it is revealed that Ghidorah is less a king and more a pawn, under the control of calculating Xiliens as part of a plan to subjugate Earth. This will become a theme.

Freed from that control, Ghidorah returns to deep space, once again falling under the thrall of an alien race in 1968's Destroy All Monsters.This movie sees Ghidorah hold its own against a who's who of kaiju, eventually succumbing to the combined might of Godzilla, Minilla, Mothra, Rodan, Manda, Gorosaurus, Anguirus, Kumonga, Baragon, and Varan.

Even with three gravity-beam-blasting heads, that is hardly fair!

Ghidorah returned with some backup for a final time in the Shōwa era in 1975's Godzilla vs. Gigan. This time, Ghidorah and his tag-team partner Gigan (who resembled a giant one-eyed space chicken) were under the control of cockroach-like Nebulans. Getting mind-controlled three times is just embarrassing!

Fortunately, the Shōwa era drew to a close in 1975 before Ghidorah was forced into servitude for a fourth time.

Ghidorah returned in the rebooted Godzilla timeline of the Heisei era (1991–1998) in 1991's Godzilla Vs. King Ghidorah, but its cosmic origins are gone. The Heisei Ghidorah begins life as three genetically engineered creatures called Dorats in the future before being morphed into a three-headed form via nuclear exposure.

Ghidorah was given a spiritual origin in the Millennium era's Godzilla, Mothra, and King Ghidorah: Giant Monsters All-Out Attack (2001), actually serving as a protagonist, helping Mothra in halting Godzilla's rampage.

Ghidorah is once again an extraterrestrial in Universal Pictures' MonsterVerse movie Godzilla: King of the Monsters (2019), being described as having "fallen from the stars" in ancient times before rising to challenge Godzilla.

Now King Ghidorah is back once again in Godzilla Minus Zero, maintaining his extraterrestrial origin story and once again challenging Godzilla to an epic kaiju battle. Those are some of the fictional versions of this golden beast, but what does it look like in reality?

Arguably the most impressive feat of King Ghidorah has been to make itself such an important part of pop culture that astronomers took note. The King Ghidorah supercluster is a collection of galaxies located around 6.7 billion light-years from Earth. Covering an area of the sky equivalent to 19 full moons, as seen from our vantage point, this cosmic Kaiju was discovered in late 2022 by Japanese astronomers using data from the Subaru Telescope.

The King Ghidorah supercluster is comprised of at least 19 individual associated galaxy clusters. It stretches out for an incredible 1.3 billion light-years, meaning the entire Milky Way could fit across it around 12,300 times. That makes it the largest supercluster ever discovered.

To put into context just how monstrous the King Ghidorah supercluster is, consider the fact that it has a mass equivalent to 10,000,000,000,000,000 suns, 10 times as massive as the Milky Way's home supercluster, Laniakea. Even Godzilla may think twice before attempting to tussle with that particular King Ghidorah!

See King Ghidorah in action in Godzilla Minus Zero, which is showing in theaters in the US and UK from November 6. You can see the preceding movie, Godzilla Minus One, on Netflix.

He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Kieran Gibbs on the challenges of moving to Major League Soccer

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The former England international headed stateside in 2021

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

After spending more than a decade in the English game, former Arsenal defender Kieran Gibbs decided he was up for a new challenge in 2021.

This saw the 10-time England international head across the Atlantic, where he signed for Inter Miami, in a move that saw him experience and embrace an entirely different footballing culture.

For Gibbs, the opportunity to work with David Beckham and Phil Neville would prove to be a key factor in his decision to up sticks, as was the chance to play a part in a league that was clearly growing.

After spending his whole career in English football, Gibbs was keen to try something new, as he recalls the circumstances that lead to his decision to move across the Atlantic.

“I felt like I wanted to try something new and different,” Gibbs tells FourFourTwo. “Once I spoke to David Beckham and Phil Neville, the game in the US and how much it’s growing appealed to me. There’s a lot of opportunities here. I’d always wanted to play in a different country.”

Gibbs would enjoy two seasons playing in Miami before hanging up his boots in 2022, but remains settled in Florida to this day.

“I’m glad that I picked here because it feels like home now,” he continues. “I go back to the UK when I can, but I’m very settled here. “I’m very grateful that I got to play football in another country and experience a different league.”

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

And while Gibbs clearly has no regrets over this move, he concedes there were initial challenges when he made the move.

“I knew it was going to be a physical game,” he admits. “People mentioned the travel, but I didn’t think that was going to be quite so difficult.

“The heat in Miami is tough as well, when you’re playing through the summer. When I moved here in July, I struggled to adapt to that.”

Gibbs also remains excited about the future of the MLS, believing that last summer’s World Cup will help the sport to continue to grow in the United States.

“It’s a growing league and there’s a lot more to come from MLS over the coming years. The World Cup this year could be a springboard for the future.

“The game has come a long way since the last World Cup here in 1994, when MLS hadn’t even been founded, so it’s still a very young sport. That’s why I’m excited to be here. I want to see how we can keep transforming the game.”

For more than a decade, Joe Mewis has worked in football journalism as a reporter and editor. Mewis has had stints at Mirror Football and LeedsLive among others and worked at FourFourTwo throughout Euro 2024, reporting on the tournament. In addition to his journalist work, Mewis is also the author of four football history books that include times on Leeds United and the England national team. Now working as a digital marketing coordinator at Harrogate Town, too, Mewis counts some of his best career moments as being in the iconic Spygate press conference under Marcelo Bielsa and seeing his beloved Leeds lift the Championship trophy during lockdown.
