The pros and cons of using an Amazfit smartwatch

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amazfit watches are easy on your wallet, but they can’t do everything.

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

Amazfit smartwatches are widely considered to be good alternatives to the bigger brands. They're generally cheaper, with some models like the well-reviewed Amazfit Bip 6 available for as little as $80. But as you would expect, there are concessions to be made to justify the lower price.

The line from the Chinese company Zepp Health includes a wide range of smartwatches to suit any lifestyle, from casual wearers to serious athletes. However, they run on a proprietary OS that limits app access, and some models have limited storage. The companion app also isn't the most intuitive.

On the plus side, Amazfit smartwatches focus on AI-powered software using its Zepp Coach and offer long battery life, with some models lasting for up to 25 days per charge. You can also find deals on Amazfit smartwatches, which are already affordable to begin with.

Before you commit, it helps to know what you're getting and what you're giving up. Here's why so many people consider Amazfit in the first place.

Amazfit smartwatches are sleek, with designs that resemble other popular models. You'll find square-shaped options like the Bip 6 and Active 2 (Square) that look like an Apple Watch. There are also round-faced ones like the Amazfit Active Max and the Amazfit T-Rex 3, which is designed for outdoor adventurers and resembles a Garmin watch in its aesthetic.

Another advantage is cross-device compatibility. You can pair an Amazfit smartwatch with either iPhone or Android since it uses its own operating system and isn't tied to either. Many trusted outlets also rank Amazfit watch models among the best budget smartwatches, citing accurate health tracking along with their stylish designs. They also note that integrations like the AI Coach, Bluetooth calling and Alexa command support are top-notch.

Amazfit smartwatches also offer over 100 sports modes, from strength training and running to gardening and Tai Chi. And they have all the important sensors you'd want for data, including heart rate, sleep, temperature, GPS and more.

Amazfit is a solid budget smartwatch brand you can continue to use even if you swap phone platforms. There's also no subscription tier for any of the data it tracks and reports, and these watches are a step up from the best cheap fitness trackers, with larger screens for at-a-glance information and control on your wrist.

Amazfit smartwatches run on the proprietary Zepp OS, so you can't access third-party apps the way you can with Wear OS watches. Instead, you're limited to a few productivity apps and games from the Zepp Store. The watches do integrate with third-party platforms like Strava, TrainingPeaks, Komoot and Runna, but you can't access Google Wallet, Google Maps, Uber, Starbucks, Gmail or banking apps. There's no Spotify app either, though you can listen on your phone and use the watch to control playback or use a remote app to mirror Spotify on the device.

Beyond apps, the software isn't as intuitive as others or as easy to navigate as those of better-known brands. You'll see unfamiliar terms like PAI, Amazfit's personal activity score and Biocharge, which is basically its equivalent of a readiness score. Some models have limited storage, which could cause issues with storing workout data or apps. The Amazfit Bip 6, for example, has 512MB of storage.

On longevity, Amazfit offers only three years of software and security support for its smartwatches. They won't stop working after that point, but it's worth factoring into the decision if you plan to hold onto the smartwatch for a while.

For a more intuitive experience with better app support, you might prefer going with affordable models from brands like Fitbit and Garmin instead, or even an older-model Samsung Galaxy Watch or Apple Watch, even if they're in their last years of software support. While Amazfit smartwatches aren't the best smartwatches you can buy, you will get reliable health tracking, long battery life and sleek designs for a great price, with a few caveats you might be able to live with.

One of the buggiest D&D games ever released, Pool of Radiance: Ruins of Myth Drannor, is back and actually playable again

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

"Not since the infamous debut of Daikatana has the release of a single game generated so much incensed indignation from early buyers." Our review of Pool of Radiance: Ruins of Myth Drannor didn't hold back. The 2001 dungeon crawler contained, our review said, "A multitude of outrageous bugs," including ones that corrupted saves, made quest items disappear, and most infamously deleted system files when you uninstalled the game.

SNEG's rerelease, available now on Steam and GOG, doesn't have those issues. It adds widescreen, controller support, adjustable combat speed, tooltips, an action bar, and sharpened visuals. If, like me, you skipped this one back in the day because of its reputation for being a hassle to play—even with the eventual patches and community fixes—now it's smooth and problem-free. I've played the opening hours without a single key item vanishing or save file going corrupt.

Don't get the wrong idea, though. Ruins of Myth Drannor isn't Vampire: The Masquerade – Bloodlines. This isn't an RPG masterpiece held back by its buggy release. It's a simplistic turn-based dungeon crawl that's mostly notable for being a spiritual successor to the first Gold Box D&D game, Pool of Radiance, and for adapting the then-new third edition of D&D's rules.

The 3.0 rules were a huge step forward at the time. Any character could choose any class, and Armor Class went up instead of down—you can't imagine how much of a drag it was explaining to new players that lower AC was better than high AC, and ideally you wanted a negative number. It also formalized the central mechanic of rolling a d20 and adding a number to beat a difficulty score. If you've played 5th edition, you'll recognize a lot of its DNA here.

Which is why it's a shame Ruins of Myth Drannor isn't a complete adaptation of those rules. You can't play a wizard—sorcerer is the only option for arcane casters—or a halfling or a gnome. You choose which class to improve when you advance a level, but don't get to decide where your skill points go or what feats to take. A couple of years later, The Temple of Elemental Evil would adapt the 3.5 rules update much more fully. Ruins of Myth Drannor feels rudimentary by comparison.

It's also not much of a story. Set 10 years after the events of Pool of Radiance, a new magical pool has opened in the ruined elven city of Myth Drannor so the evil sorceress and dracolich on the box art can get up to some shenanigans, and Elminster has sent a party of adventurers in to stop them. There's one fun twist to this otherwise bog-standard plot, and it's that you're not that first party of adventurers. You're the B-team sent as backup, but a magic portal mishap means you arrive too late to save the A-team and have to take on their quest.

Which explains why four level-one nobodies now have to clear out hundreds of orcs and skeletons from samey dungeon rooms. It's a slow-paced slog, especially in Myth Drannor's main halls, earning a drip-feed of experience points and gold coins from zombies and ghouls, hoping an orc doesn't score a crit and drop one of your party members in a single blow.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

There are plenty of reasons not to like Ruins of Myth Drannor, but I've been enjoying it in spite of them. It's an undemanding RPG that offers simple pleasures. I get ambushed by skeletons, then realize my cleric is perfectly positioned to hit them all with Turn Undead. I meet a weird slug monster, try talking to him and find out he's friendly. I block doors to protect my sorcerer, while rushing enemy spellcasters before they get their big area-of-effect blast off.

It's junk food D&D, but then I mostly play D&D at a table covered in pizza and beer anyway. Between more demanding games, this is an ideal low-stakes guilty pleasure and I'm glad it's finally back.

Jody's first computer was a Commodore 64, so he remembers having to use a code wheel to play Pool of Radiance. A former music journalist who interviewed everyone from Giorgio Moroder to Trent Reznor, Jody also co-hosted Australia's first radio show about videogames, Zed Games. He's written for Rock Paper Shotgun, The Big Issue, GamesRadar, Zam, Glixel, Five Out of Ten Magazine, and Playboy.com, whose cheques with the bunny logo made for fun conversations at the bank. Jody's first article for PC Gamer was about the audio of Alien Isolation, published in 2015. Jody edited PC Gamer Indie from 2017 to 2018, and he eventually lived up to his promise to play every Warhammer videogame.

There’s a lot of love about Season 1 of Prime Video’s popular hockey romance Off Campus. The book-to-screen adaptation has incredible chemistry, love triangles, shirtless hockey guys, and even a live performance from indie pop-rock star Remi Wolf. I’ve been spamming her music ever since the show came out, including her new album Mud. Her raspy belt is incredibly unique, and I only just found out that it actually got her pretty far on American Idol.

I’ve actually been a fan of Remi Wolf for a few years now, so I was pleasantly surprised when I started watching Off Campus, and she popped up as herself on stage in Season 1, Episode 1, which is available to stream with an Amazon Prime subscription. However, I had no idea this wasn’t her first TV appearance. Wolf was actually first introduced to audiences on Season 13 of American Idol, and recently reminisced with People about her experience on the iconic singing competition:

I got pretty decently far, and it was a cool experience to see how the entertainment industry works because I had no experience whatsoever in that world.

Wolf, who at the time was 17 years old, made it decently far in the competition after auditioning “on a whim.” She wowed judges Harry Connick Jr., Jennifer Lopez, and Keith Urban to make it to Hollywood Week before getting cut from the competition. However, the “Bottle” singer said she was grateful her time on Idol ended there:

I was such a baby, and I’m so glad that I was able to carve my own path as a baby growing into an adult rather than having that early influence on me by executives and stuff. I feel like my frontal cortex was not developed enough to handle it.

One thing I love about Remi Wolf’s music is that it’s her own unique sound, and I love that she has built her career off living completely true to herself, and that Island Records, the label she is signed to, has allowed and encouraged her to do that. I have to wonder if teenage Remi Wolf in 2014 could have predicted that her run on Idol, and eventual exit, would have led to a cameo on one of Prime Video’s most acclaimed shows of 2026.

Wolf first gained fame from her hit single “Photo I.D.” in 2021, and has since released three studio albums. Her performance in Off Campus features some of her biggest songs off her second studio album Big Ideas, including "Soup", "Toro", and "Alone In Miami."

Her latest album Mud was released on October 9 of this year, with the 30-year-old describing the project on her Instagram as “my favorite, my healer, my family, my precious, my blood, sweat, and tears.” It’s clearly her most vulnerable project to date, prompted by her decision a year and a half ago to become sober.

I would love to see Remi Wolf return to Off Campus in Season 2, which has officially been confirmed to drop on Prime April 28, 2027. The “Sexy Villain” singer said on the Zach Sang Show that she would be down to return, but that she has no idea where they would work her into the story, and I have to agree. However, I would love to hear some of her new songs off Mud included in the soundtrack when Season 2 premieres.

Since Jodie Sweetin and Andrea Barber launched their Full House rewatch podcast, How Rude, Tanneritos!, plenty of BTS sitcom tea has been spilled. From the fact that producers had a preferred Olsen twin to work with to the anger on set when Candace Cameron Bure cut her hair without warning, there's been so much to learn about the TGIF show. And, on the latest podcast episode, it was revealed that one actor had “too big a pack” to be wearing biker shorts for a scene with Kimmy Gibbler, and this sounds so cringey.

In the season 7 episode of “Love on the Rocks,” DJ Tanner debates whether or not to break up with her boyfriend, Steve Hale. When she goes mountain climbing with Kimmy and her boyfriend, Keanu, she gains a new perspective on her life and relationship. While discussing the episode, in a recent episode of How Rude, Tanneritos!, Kimmy actress Andrea Barber exposed a rather revealing detail about her on-screen boyfriend that cropped up amid filming:

This poor actor, Keanu. So originally, he wasn’t wearing his jacket and it was just his shirt and his biker shorts. But, the bike shorts were a little tight. And a little too revealing.

Yes, poor actor! When you rewatch the episode, the biker shorts Keanu was wearing really did look tight, almost like spandex. And for a family-friendly show, it makes sense to cover that up. Like Sweetin humorously said, that’s just too much information for families at home to see.

It’s common for wardrobe changes to happen when an actor on a family-friendly sitcom is showing a little growth down there. That happened to Jaleel White when he was asked to hide his bulge in Family Matters. In the case of Brian Evans, who played Keanu, Barber explained that the wardrobe department had other ideas to hide that area:

They got a note. Costumes got a note from Video Village. And so, they had to quickly find this jacket. A long jacket to cover.

Well, I’ve got to say, the long jacket worked. You can’t see anything. Hey, you do what you have to do to keep the Full House episode G-rated.

Anyone curious as to how Brian Evans felt about the embarrassing wardrobe change should know he told EW that he “didn’t know what was happening.” Not only that, but he also revealed the priceless reaction his mother had when one of the crew members told him he had “too big of a pack" for a teenager on this show”:

I remember seeing my mother’s face sitting at the studio audience just beet red, laughing.

This certainly isn't the kind of moment I would've expected to hear regarding the long-binge-watchable show, but it's also a funny piece of information. Thank goodness the costume department of the ABC sitcom was right on top of it. Otherwise, I could imagine the producers receiving some not-so-flattering letters from parents.

It’ll be hard not to think about this little (or not-so-little) tidbit of information when watching this Full House episode moving forward. Still, the detail shouldn't be too distracting for audiences given how the jacket comes into play. Fans can watch “Love on the Rocks” and more episodes of the show by streaming them with either a Hulu subscription or a Disney+ subscription.

Just your average South Floridian cinephile who believes the pen is mightier than the sword.

Magnite vs. Reddit: Which Media Stock Is a Better Buy Going Into 2027?

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Reddit's revenue surged far faster than Magnite's last year, yet only one of these ad stocks trades at a meaningfully lower forward earnings multiple.

As digital advertising shifts away from traditional formats, investors must decide which platforms can best navigate the changing landscape. Choosing between Magnite (MGNI -0.47%) and Reddit (RDDT +1.38%) depends on your preferred growth strategy.

Magnite serves as a vital middleman for publishers looking to sell digital ad space, while Reddit offers a massive community-driven platform for direct engagement. Both companies represent different segments of the modern advertising ecosystem.

Magnite operates as the world's largest independent sell-side advertising company. It provides the technology that helps publishers monetize their content across connected TV, online video, and display formats. The company serves major partners like LG Electronics-owned LG Ad Solutions, and recently partnered with OpenAI to boost ad placements within ChatGPT.

In its latest annual report, filed for its 2025 fiscal year (FY), revenue reached nearly $714.0 million. This represents revenue growth of 6.9% compared to the previous year. Magnite reported net income of $144.6 million for the period, which resulted in a net margin of 20.3%.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the debt-to-equity ratio was 0.7x. This metric compares total debt to shareholder equity to show how much a company relies on borrowing. The current ratio, which measures the ability to cover short-term debts with short-term assets, stands at 1.0x. Free cash flow was $165.6 million. Note that stock-based compensation (SBC) represented 32.5% of operating cash flow, which inflates reported cash generation since SBC is a non-cash expense added back in the cash flow statement.

Reddit is a prominent name for those investing in social media stocks. It generates most of its revenue from third-party advertising, but it has also expanded into content licensing for training generative artificial intelligence models. The platform reported 130.3 million daily active unique (DAUq) users as of the third quarter of 2026, showing its massive reach.

In its latest annual report, filed for FY 2025, revenue reached $2.2 billion. This reflects significant revenue growth of 69.4% over the prior year. Reddit reported net income of $529.7 million, delivering a net margin of 24.1% as it transitioned into profitability.

As of its December 2025 balance sheet, the company carried a debt-to-equity ratio of zero. Its current ratio was 11.6x, indicating a strong ability to meet short-term obligations. Free cash flow reached $684.2 million for the year. Note that stock-based compensation represented 49.7% of operating cash flow.

Magnite faces significant revenue concentration, with two buyers indirectly contributing about 44% of its total revenue. Its success depends heavily on the growth of the connected TV market and continued access to that inventory. The company also deals with intense competition from Alphabet (GOOGL +0.78%), Amazon (AMZN -0.35%), and Comcast (CMCSA -2.24%). Furthermore, ongoing litigation with Alphabet's Google regarding anticompetitive practices remains a potential financial risk.

Reddit relies on its ability to maintain user engagement and contributions, which are the primary drivers of its advertising value. The company faces concentration risks, as its top ten advertisers account for roughly 21% of total revenue. Its community-led moderation model can lead to disruptions that may alienate advertisers or users. Additionally, Reddit depends on infrastructure provided by third-party cloud services from Amazon and Alphabet to remain operational.

Reddit stock trades at a lower multiple of future earnings estimates, while Magnite appears more attractively priced based on its sales over the past twelve months.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

Between Magnite and Reddit, I would prioritize investing in Reddit. Both rely on digital advertising to fund their businesses, but Reddit's consumer-facing community platform gives it the edge.

The company's 130.3 million DAUq users in Q3 represented strong 12% year-over-year growth, indicating Reddit continues to attract an engaged user base. This makes the social media giant a compelling place for advertisers to spend their budgets. Consequently, the company grew Q3 revenue by a whopping 53% year over year to $895 million.

Reddit stock is also much cheaper based on its forward earnings multiple, and while its sales multiple is a bit higher than Magnite's, the company's much faster revenue growth signals that it's business is still rapidly expanding. Magnite's Q3 sales of $204.2 million represented an excellent 14% year-over-year increase, but is still much lower than Reddit's 53%.

The one important downside to note is that Reddit's reliance on search engines to deliver traffic to its site is at risk with the artificial intelligence boom. Users are increasingly looking to AI when performing searches, bringing a drop to traditional search visits. Reddit is responding by testing a requirement for users to log in when visiting the site, which prevents AI from taking its content.

Robert Izquierdo has positions in Alphabet, Amazon, Comcast, Magnite, and Reddit. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet, Amazon, and Reddit. The Motley Fool recommends Comcast and Magnite. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Derek McInnes pinpoints Rangers biggest strength as St Mirren fume over penalty award and VAR consistency

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Derek McInnes insists Rangers are heading in the right direction after extending their unbeaten domestic run to 12 matches with a comfortable 2-0 victory over St Mirren in Paisley.

Goals in either half from Ryan Naderi and captain Dan Neil secured another valuable three points for the Ibrox side, who moved to within a point of league leaders Celtic, with a game in hand still to play after this weekend.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Despite acknowledging that his team could have been more clinical in front of goal, McInnes was delighted with the way his players approached what he considers one of the more difficult away assignments in the Scottish Premiership.

“I think this is a hard place to come for any team, but for our club as well, over the years it's never easy and we didn't expect it to be anything other than that,” McInnes said. “But I thought we dealt with the game really well. I thought we were really competitive from the get-go, I thought we were tenacious, we were aggressive, we moved the ball right.

“We just came back to that last part of it not being clinical enough. Quite ironic, we scored two goals tonight from a penalty kick and a deflected shot at the edge of the box, given all the chances we had, but I actually thought in the main it was no more than we deserved.”

Naderi was once again central to Rangers' attacking efforts, taking his season's tally to nine goals and five assists after confidently dispatching his first-half spot-kick.

McInnes was particularly impressed by the striker's work rate and physical presence, believing the forward has taken significant strides in recent weeks as he looks to establish himself as a dependable option to lead the line.

“I think he's so willing. His first port of call was to make sure he's a handful to play against and I thought he was that,” McInnes stated. “He needs to build that reputation for being horrible to play against, but equally at a club like Rangers you're going to get judged on the goals.

“He's totally shown that he's somebody you can depend on, somebody that can be trusted, somebody that you can rely on to get goals as well as bring in good performances.”

McInnes was equally thrilled with goalkeeper Ivor Pandur, who produced several important saves to preserve Rangers' clean sheet as St Mirren threatened a comeback in the closing stages.

“I think he's been very clean, very controlled with what he does. His distribution is brilliant, he makes good decisions,” he said. “I think there's so much potential in Ivor. With a rotten goalie, you're not winning nothing. He is somebody who we really take a lot of comfort from.

“I've played with goalkeepers who were brilliant and I've played with some who weren't so good and you never ever felt comfortable. With him, we do. He's been a big part of the amount of clean sheets we've had. I thought we worked hard for a clean sheet tonight but there was a spell in the second half where the space got a wee bit bigger and St Mirren managed to get a few opportunities. Thankfully, you always feel he's got that save in him.”

Saints have now claimed just one win in their last nine games and boss Craig McLeish was left frustrated by the first-half penalty award, questioning the consistency of the decisions made by officials following Samuel Ramos’ challenge on James Penrice.

“My thoughts on the penalty... If that's the criteria for contact in the box, then fine,” he said. “But Kabs (Ismeal Kabia) gets wiped out in the second half after he puts a cross in. I don't even know if anyone looks at it.

“We get an apology after the game at Ibrox for a penalty that we should have got on Kabs again where there was a decent amount of contact on it. I get that if the ref gives it on the field, then it's maybe difficult for VAR to overturn it. I think if he doesn't give it there and then, I don't think VAR gets involved to give it.

“It's a difficult one and it's a frustrating one from our point of view, but there's also bits leading up to it getting to that situation that we need to deal with better. I need to be careful with what I'm saying. It's a frustrating one from our point of view.”

Asked whether he had spoken to Nick Walsh after the final whistle to seek clarification, McLeish replied bluntly: “No, there's no point.”

Claude Personal Finance vs. ChatGPT Finances: What sets them apart?

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Both AI tools offer to delve deep into your bank accounts, but they differ in privacy settings.

Ahead of its plans to go public with a potentially record-breaking valuation, Anthropic recently announced a new feature for Claude called Personal Finance. It's an obvious rival to OpenAI's Finances feature within ChatGPT, which debuted in May. Both options from these AI giants are meant to provide users with a better understanding of their finances and are not designed to offer expert financial advice, but there are slight variations in how they do it that might make you lean towards one or the other. To help you decide which AI chatbot to go with for more clarity on your finances, we've laid out the major similarities and differences of Claude's Personal Finance and ChatGPT's Finances features.

Covering a broad spectrum of personal finance topics, there's a lot of crossover in what both OpenAI and Anthropic's offerings can do. Both of their AI chatbots can address general questions about financial goals, like buying a house, planning an expensive family vacation or creating a savings plan. Claude and ChatGPT will also generate dashboards to offer users better visuals at things like monthly expenses, investments and spending habits.

To activate either Claude's Personal Finance tool or ChatGPT's Finances feature, you have to link your bank account. Both AI companies partnered with Plaid to make this happen, allowing connections with the most popular financial institutions, like Bank of America, Capital One or Chase. Once set up, both Claude and ChatGPT only have "read-only" access to your financial accounts, meaning they can only view the info provided and would not be able to make any changes.

Anthropic specified that Claude's Personal Finance feature can only see your bank name, account name, account type, last four account digits, balances, credit limits and up to two years of transactions. Similarly, OpenAI said that its Finances tool can see balances, transactions, investments and liabilities.

While the core function of Anthropic's Personal Finance and OpenAI's Finances are very similar, the major difference between the two has to do with privacy settings. By default, Anthropic said, "when you chat with Claude in the Personal Finance hub, those conversations aren't used to train our models." Anthropic added that Claude will no longer see any data that you previously provided to its Personal Finance feature if you unlink your bank account.

On the other hand, OpenAI said that "your conversations with connected financial accounts follow the same model training settings you choose across ChatGPT." In this case, you have to opt out of your data being used by navigating to Data controls in ChatGPT's settings and turning off the option called Improve the model for everyone, which is on by default. When you disconnect your bank accounts from OpenAI, the company said that "your synced account data will be deleted from OpenAI's systems within 30 days."

To differentiate itself from OpenAI's alternative, Anthropic said that its Personal Finance feature "has no ads or sponsored recommendations, and it doesn't offer credit cards, loans or savings accounts of its own." While OpenAI doesn't explicitly mention ads with ChatGPT Finances, the company has been testing out ads for its cheaper tiers as of January.

OpenAI said it's looking to bring more features to ChatGPT's Finances by partnering with Intuit to cover more bases like credit card applications or tax questions. As for Anthropic's next steps with Personal Finance, the company wants to hear from banks, wealth managers and financial services companies to help improve the AI tool.

→ Baca artikel asli

Mortgage rates fell and demand steadied as inventory dipped

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Inventory declined week over week to 902,740 as mortgage rates eased for 3 straight weeks. With spreads at 2.27%, the path to sub-7% mortgage rates can happen with lower bond yields or better mortgage spreads.  AI Summary

Mortgage rates fell, demand stabilized and inventory had a noticeable decline last week. But are the changes in demand and inventory data about seasonality, or has the housing market changed with the recent mortgage rate decline? Let’s take a look at the weekend tracker and see what the data is telling us with rates falling the last three weeks.

In the 2026 HousingWire forecast, I anticipated the following ranges:

What a crazy time in the bond market recently: the key level of 5.35% has not only held since early October, but the reversal has been truly nice to watch. The midterm elections, which changed the control of the House and Senate, have added another variable into the mortgage rate equation. I discussed what Congress can do to help lower mortgage rates on this episode of the HousingWire Daily podcast with Editor in Chief Sarah Wheeler, but for now the head-and-shoulders pattern of the 10-year yield is still somewhat intact, though a fight for it to break lower is happening.

I wouldn’t get too excited about mortgage rates until the 10-year yield can break under 4.70% and have follow-through bond buying, but mortgage rates have fallen and demand has stabilized over the last few weeks.

Mortgage spreads have worsened recently, but they’re still trending near multiyear lows. Traditionally, when mortgage rates escalate with the 10-year yield and then make a big reversal — like we just had from a 5.35% 10-year yield top that dropped down toward 4.82% —  spreads do get worse to try to compress mortgage rate volatility.That said, if mortgage spreads weren’t near multiyear lows right now, we’d have no chance of getting mortgage rates below 7.25%, and demand wouldn’t have stabilized recently. Now that spreads have moved up from this year’s lows, we can clearly see that mortgage rates would be sub-7% if spreads return to that level.

Historically, mortgage spreads have ranged from 1.60% to 1.80%. Last week, spreads rose to 2.27% from 2.23% a week earlier.

Let’s compare last week’s mortgage rates to where they would have been at the best spread levels this year and the worst levels of this cycle.

Our pending home sales data provides a week-to-week perspective, though holidays and short-term fluctuations can affect results. This weekly pending sales data typically takes 30-60 days to reflect in sales data.

The key for the last three weeks: lower rates, even above 7%, have stabilized demand, as our weekly pending sales have shown. Now, this isn’t growth, just stabilization from a noticeable slowdown when mortgage rates got toward 7.60%. The last three years and 10 months show that housing demand grows when mortgage rates fall below 6.64% and head toward 6%; above 7%, we don’t have much growth data beyond seasonal demand. Last year at this time, mortgage rates were headed toward 6%, so the year-over-year comps will be hard for some time.

Here are the pending sales for last week over the last two years:

Purchase application data, which looks out 30-90 days, has softened as mortgage rates rose above 6.64% and are now above 7%. We had a small decline week to week; I can see the stabilization here as well and the year-over-year comps will also be very difficult here, as last year at this time rates were heading toward 6%. Earlier in the year, when rates were lower, pretty much every week this year showed growth.

Here are the stats on purchase apps so far in 2026:

Housing inventory growth has been very tame this year, with some weeks being down year over year. However, when mortgage rates got above 6.64% and then headed toward 7.60%, we did see some growth pick up. Also, rates were lower last year so demand was heading toward a nine-month high in December, which means it the comps will be easier to show growth in inventory.

Inventory growth is running at 7.18% even with easier comps.

New listings are in their typical seasonal decline; 2026 has been the healthiest new-listings year since 2022, with over 80,000 new listings per week a few times this year. The concern I had was that if higher rates cause sellers to stop listing their homes — as they did in the second half of 2022 — that would hurt supply and demand. However, that hasn’t happened in any meaningful way. Most home sellers are buyers, and now that rates have fallen a smidge, it looks like we can end the year on a positive note for 2026 for new listings.

In a normal environment, new listings range from 80,000 to 100,000 per week during peak periods. For context, during the housing bubble years, new listings ranged from 250,000 to 400,000 per week for several years.Here is last week’s new listings data for the past two years:

Typically, about one-third of homes see price reductions before they sell, reflecting the housing market’s dynamic nature. Overall, price-cut percentages this year have been lower than last year. This held true until rates moved above 6.64% and then above 7%; demand fell, and the year-over-year price-cut percentage rose 1%.In my 2026 home-price forecast, I called for a national decline of -0.62% for the year. Home-price growth really isn’t going anywhere this year, and my -0.62% forecast may be hard to achieve, since most home-price indexes show growth between 1% and 2%. However, with higher rates and a rising price-cut percentage, we will be closer to the forecast. Clearly, if rates hadn’t risen above 6.75%, pricing data wouldn’t have shown the recent slowdown.

You want to keep an eye on how AI stocks trade this week, in particular CoreWeave’s stock price and how the 10-year yield reacts. For now, there’s an interesting correlation between the two, but I expect it to fade over time.

It’s inflation week again, with both CPI and PPI reports. Over the next 12 months, if the Iran conflict ends and the trade war doesn’t get worse, the question is whether bond traders will put more weight on those two factors than on the current inflation reports, which could improve in the future. This would be a huge positive for mortgage rates, but still, it will take a lot of work to end the trade war and the conflict. We also have a lot of Fed speeches this week and it’s critical now to see what they say and how the bond market reacts to their talking points. Also, with the midterms over, I will be watching whether any of them start to talk about the end of the conflict and trade war being a positive for their future outlook.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/weekly-rundown-hims-hers-adds-new-partners-benefits-program-hippocratic-ai-debuts" hreflang="en">Weekly Rundown: Hims &amp; Hers adds new partners for benefits program; Hippocratic unveils updates to healthcare-focused AI model</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Weekly Rundown: Hims & Hers adds new partners for benefits program; Hippocratic unveils updates to healthcare-focused AI model" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Stay up to date on the latest in health tech, digital health and health AI news with this weekly brief. Read below for news from the week of Nov. 2 to 6.

Hims & Hers announced Thursday the addition of four new partners to its benefits program, bringing the total of included partners to 14.

The new partners are nutrition gummy maker Grüns; air medical transport membership service Medjet; AI-powered home strength system Tonal and full body intelligence provider Viome.

Superscript is opening access to its platform through the launch of a new API, which allows any healthcare practice or electronic health record system to access real-time healthcare pricing information.

The platform, backed by Mark Cuban and Nava Ventures, is currently live with 1,500 active healthcare providers.  To date, it has served more than 500,000 patients and processed 2.2 million patient billing transactions, executives say.

One of the organizations Superscript works with is Michigan-based Orthopaedic Associates of Muskegon (OAM).

“Patients deserve certainty not only in their clinical diagnosis and treatment plans, but also in their financial obligations,” said Jeff Cave, OAM CEO, in a statement. “Historically, doctors and administrative teams had no way of knowing exact insurance reimbursement rates at the point of care. Partnering with Superscript lets our patients know exactly what they owe before their visit. That transparency builds trust, eliminates anxiety, and allows our care team to focus entirely on delivering high-quality care.”

Hippocratic AI launched Monday a new generation of its healthcare language model that features six new upgrades to its AI agents.

The company says the new version of its healthcare-focused AI model can conduct more natural and responsive patient conversations by interpreting emotional cues, reasoning while speaking and adapting its voice in real time, while improving safety, speed and electronic health record system integration.

The upgrade—dubbed Polaris 6 Ultra—features what executives call “bedside intuition,” a first-of-its-kind capability that reads patients’ emotional state and decides next speech without pause.

“In healthcare, you don't get credit for reminding a patient. You get credit for the action they take,” said Munjal Shah, Hippocratic AI CEO and co-founder, in a statement. “Polaris 6.0 was built from more than 323 million patient conversations, making it the only truly experienced frontier model for healthcare able to operate safely and autonomously to drive patient outcomes. That experience shows. Polaris 6.0 handles the healthcare edge cases of real life, it now has more human-like common sense and pacing, and it delivers the kind of empathy that persuades patients.”

Other capabilities include AI intuition, an upgraded version of Care Voice and enhanced Common Sense.

Polaris 6 Ultra also runs on 7.4 trillion parameters and delivers a 1.3X end-to-end speedup compared to Polaris 5, the company says.

A coalition of organizations is calling on the U.S. Drug Enforcement Administration (DEA) to extend its telemedicine prescribing flexibilities deadline given the lack of finalization to a key rule.

The flexibilities are set to expire on Dec. 31, and the agency’s Special Registrations for Telemedicine and Limited State Telemedicine Registrations final rule has not been published.

The coalition said patients and providers are “facing unacceptable uncertainty and the real risk of care disruptions,” noting that providers and platforms will need time to review requirements and apply for registration even if the rule is published in November.

“Patients should not have to wonder in December whether their January appointments will happen—or whether they will lose access to medications they rely on,” said Chris Adamec, Alliance for Connected Care executive director, in a statement. “With 59 days remaining and no published final rule, DEA can give providers and patients the stability they need by issuing a fifth extension as soon as possible.”

Advocates are calling for an extension through at least Dec. 31, 2027.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/hims-hers-inks-deal-eli-lilly-offer-branded-weight-loss-drugs" hreflang="en">Hims &amp; Hers inks deal with Eli Lilly to offer branded weight loss drugs</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Hims & Hers inks deal with Eli Lilly to offer branded weight loss drugs" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Hims & Hers struck a deal with Eli Lilly to offer the drugmaker’s branded obesity treatments through its online health and wellness platform.

Under the agreement, Eli Lilly will give Hims & Hers pharmacies direct access to its U.S. Food and Drug Administration-approved GLP-1 medications at self-pay prices. Eligible patients on the Hims & Hers platform can be prescribed and order Eli Lilly's injectable Zepbound (tirzepatide) and daily oral medication Foundayo (orforglipron) without leaving the company's platform.

Prior to this agreement, Lilly drugs were available to patients on Hims & Hers only if they left the site and went to Lilly's direct-to-consumer platform, LillyDirect.

Shares of Hims & Hers jumped 8% Friday following news of the Eli Lilly partnership.

The agreement brings together Lilly’s obesity medicines and Hims & Hers’ consumer platform to expand consumers' access to FDA-approved weight loss drugs, the companies said.

"Patients can complete a consultation, receive a prescription if their provider determines it is appropriate and have an FDA-approved Lilly medicine shipped directly to their home, all in one seamless experience," the companies said in a press release.

Self-pay pricing starts at $149 per month for Foundayo and $299 per month for Zepbound.

"At Lilly, we are always looking for new ways to help more people get the medicines they need, and that means meeting patients where they already are," said Ilya Yuffa, executive vice president and president, Lilly USA and global customer capabilities, Eli Lilly and Company, in a statement. "Millions of people turn to Hims & Hers for their care, and through this agreement, those patients now have a simple, more direct way to get FDA-approved Lilly medicine at an affordable price if prescribed."

Hims & Hers' technology platform supports provider outreach, follow-up care and treatment planning. Weight loss patients on Hims & Hers receive a membership experience that includes 24/7 access to a care team, nutrition guidance, regular clinical check-ins and access to the Hims & Hers Weight Loss community, according to the company.

The agreement with Eli Lilly mirrors Hims & Hers' partnership with Novo Nordisk, which has been selling its branded weight loss drugs Wegovy and Ozempic through Hims & Hers since March. Novo had previously sued the telehealth company for offering compounded versions of its medications. Hims & Hers' agreement with Novo marked a shift in its obesity strategy toward FDA-approved branded GLP-1 treatments and away from cheaper compounded GLP-1 drugs.

Earlier this year, Hims & Hers took heat from the pharmaceutical manufacturer and federal regulators after announcing plans to launch a knockoff of Novo's oral pill. It backed down from those plans under pressure.

Hims & Hers stated that it never offered access to compounded copies of Lilly’s GLP-1 drugs and has committed not to do so in the future.

As part of the agreement, the companies have committed to protecting independent clinical decision-making, so that providers on the Hims & Hers platform continue to prescribe the treatment they determine is right for each patient.

“Lilly is one of the most innovative pharmaceutical companies in history, and I’m incredibly proud that Hims & Hers is a part of making sure more people can access the medicines they’ve created,” said Andrew Dudum, co-founder and CEO of Hims & Hers, in a statement. “By working together, we’re not just reaching more people with obesity with seamless, personal access to FDA-approved Lilly medicine. We’re showing the industry what innovators can achieve for customers everywhere when we work together to make the world well.”

Hims & Hers has faced financial pressure as it shifts from high-margin compounded GLP-1s to lower-margin branded weight-loss drugs. The company posted a $92 million loss in Q1, including $33 million in restructuring costs, largely from write-downs of its compounded GLP-1 supply chain, which executives said faced “risk of obsolescence.”

In Q2, Hims & Hers reported an $86 million loss, compared with a $43 million profit a year earlier. The quarter included $81 million in one-time costs, including expenses tied to its exit from compounded GLP-1s.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/finance/omada-health-hikes-2026-outlook-after-strongest-quarter-ever-q3" hreflang="en">Omada Health hikes 2026 outlook after ‘strongest quarter ever’ in Q3</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Omada Health hikes 2026 outlook after ‘strongest quarter ever’ in Q3" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Omada Health saw its "strongest quarter ever" in the third quarter of 2026, CEO and co-founder Sean Duffy told investors and analysts during a Thursday evening earnings call.

"We delivered our highest quarterly revenue, gross margin progression, and our most profitable quarter to date," Duffy said.

The virtual chronic care provider, which went public last June, brought in $91 million in revenue—up 34% year-over-year—and reported gross margin of 76% on a GAAP basis and 78% on a non-GAAP basis, according to Duffy. Omada reported net income of $7 million in the third quarter, compared with a net loss of $3 million in Q3 2025.

The company also reported adjusted EBITDA of $12 million, compared to $2 million the year prior.

It beat Wall Street analysts’ estimates for revenue, though slightly trailed behind projections for total membership.

Total members reached 1.1 million, up 36% year-over-year, with trailing 12-month revenue per total member increasing 5% year-over-year to $294, according to financial results published Thursday. Average member tenure also grew 10% compared to the year prior, to approximately two years and four months. Member retention has improved to 58%, up from 43% in Q4 of 2024, executives said.

Omada Health President Wei-Li Shao said Q3 membership growth reflects "continued demand" for its cardiometabolic platform, adding that the company's diabetes and hypertension programs continued to lead growth.

The company's diabetes and hypertension programs, longer member retention and lower care delivery costs are increasingly driving both growth and profitability, executives said.

"Members stayed with us longer, and our ability to provide personalized care at scale reached a new level of efficiency," Shao said.

The company supports diabetes, hypertension, obesity and musculoskeletal conditions—launching a GLP-1 care companion solution last November.

Shao noted that Omada deepened its partnership with the Health Care Services Corporation (HCSC) last quarter to extend its prevention and hypertension programs into three additional states, which is expected to "potentially reach an additional estimated 1.5 million covered lives at launch in 2027."

"On the PBM side, we have commercial relationships with the three largest PBMs in the country, which together processed an estimated 80% of prescription claims in 2025," Shao said. "Our prescribing program is in the market now at Optum. These relationships give us the broadest distribution in Omada’s history for our programs."

Shao, who will take over as CEO Jan. 1, also noted that artificial intelligence solutions enable care teams to have more capacity to "apply their clinical expertise" while tailoring care to each member’s needs.

"This quarter shows what we have built at Omada," Shao said. "Our commercial relationships give us broad reach, and our cardiometabolic programs have driven growth. Our program mix has supported longer member tenure, and our cost to serve has decreased."

Chief Financial Officer Steve Cook told investors the company expects new enrollments to "moderate sequentially" in the fourth quarter, which he said is "consistent with historic seasonality."

Shao also noted that a number of employers have been spending more time making GLP-1 coverage and obesity care decisions, "given the size of the commitment, the range of solutions and the importance of getting these decisions right," which has extended the selling season deeper into the fourth quarter.

Omada Health's stock dropped 19% Friday following the Q3 earnings call on Thursday as investors pulled back due to concerns about the longer selling season and expected Q4 member decline clouding the company's 2027 outlook.

The company is raising its full 2026 revenue and adjusted EBITDA outlook. Cook said revenue is expected to range from $344 million to $346 million, up from its previous range of $334 million to $340 million. Adjusted EBITDA is expected to be in the range of $32 million to $34 million, up from $21 million to $27 million.

"Our outlook anticipates Q4 revenue below our Q3 level, reflecting the lower new enrollment and engagement activity we have historically seen during the holidays," Cook said. "We expect revenue to come primarily from continued engagement among existing members."

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/ai-and-machine-learning/equiti-acquires-lexi-ai-combine-ai-human-medical-interpretation" hreflang="en">Equiti acquires Lexi AI to combine AI, human medical interpretation</a>

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Equiti acquires Lexi AI to combine AI, human medical interpretation" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Equiti is acquiring Lexi AI to build a hybrid language-access model that combines AI language support with human medical interpreters.

Equiti, which sells medical interpretation services to hospitals and health systems through its Martti business, provides 24/7 access to qualified medical interpreters across more than 250 languages and dialects. Lexi provides multilingual, AI-powered medical interpretation. The acquisition deal merges Lexi's immediate AI language support, covering over 35 languages and dialects, with Martti and its network of qualified medical interpreters to help healthcare organizations expand language access.

The acquisition was announced October 28. Financial details of the deal were not disclosed.

With this acquisition, Equiti is building on Martti’s work pioneering video remote interpretation in healthcare more than two decades ago, according to executives. The use of AI can expand where and when language services can be offered, particularly for everyday interactions that may not currently receive interpretation support, Equiti executives said.

Martti was built alongside healthcare organizations to make qualified interpreters available by video. Lexi, in turn, has been developed with healthcare customers to extend immediate language support into more workflows. The acquisition brings those capabilities together, with human-in-the-loop design, patient safety and patient experience at its center, Equiti executives said.

The company saw an opportunity to combine its services with Lexi's AI expertise rather than build a healthcare AI interpretation product internally, Maureen Huber, Equiti CEO, told Fierce Healthcare.

"Lexi brings specialized expertise in AI, engineering and healthcare product design, along with experience putting that technology into the hands of care teams. That combination takes time to build," Huber said. "For more than 20 years, our work through Martti has been about making qualified human interpretation accessible within healthcare. We understand the service, the clinical environment and what it takes to support healthcare organizations every day. Developing AI and machine learning technology calls for additional, specialized expertise. We wanted people who had already been doing that work with healthcare in mind."

She added, "We spent time evaluating the category and learning from customers. Lexi brought together the people, product and approach we were seeking. Combining their expertise with ours gives us a stronger foundation to expand language access responsibly."

Combining AI and human interpretation makes language support more accessible across the patient journey, helping organizations improve care delivery while making better use of their resources, according to Equiti executives.

Healthcare organizations are increasingly interested in AI interpretation capabilities and are looking for guidance, Huber noted.

"Customers were asking: 'Where could AI help? How do we evaluate it? What is appropriate for our patients?' Healthcare leaders see the same advances in consumer AI that everyone else sees. They also must think about what happens when those tools enter a care environment, sometimes before an organization has decided how they should be used. They want a partner who understands both the possibilities and the responsibilities," she said.

She added, "I would describe that as inquiry and evaluation. There is real curiosity about extending language support to more of the patient experience, particularly before and after a visit. Our job is to help customers identify those opportunities while keeping qualified human interpretation central to care delivery."

Equiti calls this approach Modality Matters, a connected model that allows healthcare organizations to match each encounter with the appropriate modality — immediate, healthcare-purpose-built AI support for approved use cases and qualified medical interpretation where clinical complexity, regulatory requirements, organizational policy or patient preference calls for a human interpreter. With this approach, organizations have the flexibility to shift between modalities as clinical context and communication needs change in real time.

The combined portfolio of Equiti and Lexi's capabilities will extend language access into everyday interactions that often go unsupported, Huber noted.

Equiti is focusing Lexi's AI-powered medical interpretation on routine patient engagement, including before and after care, such as appointment communications, scheduling and nonclinical follow-up logistics.

"We see a significant opportunity for Lexi in that communication before and after care: helping patients exchange messages, arrange appointments and stay connected in their preferred language. Language access must extend beyond the clinical encounter. Think about what happens after a patient leaves the clinic. They receive a message about a follow-up appointment, but they need to ask whether it is at the same location, how to reschedule, or whom to contact. Those may be simple questions for the organization, but the answers determine whether the patient can take the next step," she said.

Qualified human interpreters remain the foundation for care delivery, Huber noted. "In conversations such as those about a diagnosis, informed consent, treatment or palliative care, the ability to clarify meaning, recognize cultural context and respond with empathy is essential. A medically qualified interpreter helps people understand one another through conversations that can be complex and deeply personal," she said.

Equiti said it can work with customers to define appropriate uses within their policies and applicable requirements, preserving patient choice and access to a qualified human interpreter.

Language access is also a strategic performance driver for healthcare organizations, as effective communication influences patient understanding and experience, clinical outcomes, workforce efficiency and financial performance.

Equiti's customers include Wellstar Health System, Corewell Health, NYU Langone and Lee Health. Lexi works with a growing pipeline of federally qualified health centers and community providers including Lowell Community Health Center, which serves one of the most multilingual communities in Massachusetts.

As general-purpose AI gets better at translation, a healthcare-specific interpretation platform has distinct advantages, Huber asserted.

"Healthcare communication involves clinical meaning, cultural context, jargon and acronyms during situations where people may be frightened, vulnerable or unsure how to ask a question. Our job is to remove those communication barriers, and that requires understanding the care environment, fitting into clinical workflows and knowing when a qualified human interpreter needs to be involved," she said. "That is where Equiti’s experience matters. We bring the relationships, service infrastructure and human expertise developed through more than two decades of healthcare interpretation, alongside Lexi’s technology and team."

Huber said she envisions broader uses of Lexi's AI language support in healthcare workflows. "A patient’s need to communicate continues after the conversation with a clinician ends. Lexi already supports text-based messaging, including translation of text provided by a care team, and can even leave voicemail messages for patients. That creates opportunities to help patients access information and communicate between visits. Helping someone navigate follow-up care is a natural direction for that work, like understanding the next step, connecting with the right office or arranging an appointment. The value is in making it easier for patients to act on the care plan they have been given and ask for help along the way," she said.

"We built Lexi to fit the way healthcare teams actually work, bringing immediate language support into the devices and workflows they already use,” Siddharth UR, co-founder and chief product officer of Lexi, said in a statement. “As part of Equiti, we can extend that experience across the patient journey and connect it to a broader language access model built around the needs of each encounter.”

Lexi will operate alongside Martti under Equiti, with the teams working together on implementation and integration across healthcare workflows. Equiti will work with each customer to determine how the solutions fit within its language access program, clinical environment, and organizational policies.

Cesc Fabregas, tecnico del Como, intervenuto ai microfoni di Sky Sport dopo lo strabiliante vittoria per 9-1 contro il Lens, ha parlato così: "E' una serata felice, questo è il calcio: oggi è andato tutto dalla nostra parte. Bravi ragazzi, ora pensiamo ad una partita molto importante domenica: dobbiamo finire come possiamo al meglio. E' una bella serata per il Como e per i ragazzi: però piedi a terra, umiltà e continuare a lavorare. Dobbiamo imparare ma non per fare il fenomeno, ma per dire che continuiamo così a fare belle prestazioni.

Gol subito? Sì sono arrabbiato, abbiamo cambiato qualcosina: l'ha fatto un mio ex compagno e non l'ho mai visto fare un gol così. Quando ho visto che le ultime due erano PSG e Barcellona ho pensato che dovevamo fare più punti all'inizio: sono due squadre superiori. Mancano tante partite a gennaio: penso a recuperare tanti giocatori che mancano. E' questa la cosa più importante, in attacco mancano in tanti. Dobbiamo fare l'ultimo sprint domenica provando a fare una bella partita e poi riposarsi, ne abbiamo bisogno".

A San Siro arriva il Como, il retroscena su Nico Paz: “Ai dirigenti Inter ha detto queste parole"

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A San Siro arriva il Como, il retroscena su Nico Paz: “Ai dirigenti Inter ha detto queste parole"

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

6 gol e 8 assist in 13 partite totali tra Serie A e Champions League: numeri da urlo per Nico Paz in questa prima parte di stagione col Como. In estate il suo nome era stato accostato all’Inter soprattutto a inizio mercato. Ma cosa c’era di vero?

A mercato chiuso, Fabrizio Romano ha svelato quanto segue proprio su Nico Paz e l’Inter. “Nico Paz la scorsa estate doveva rientrare al Real Madrid, ma il Como ci ha sempre creduto nel riuscire a contare sulla sua volontà. Posso rivelarvi che Nico ha detto in una chiamata diretta con Jose Mourinho: "Grazie mister per la stima ma voglio restare al Como, penso sia fondamentale per giocarle tutte e crescere". Mourinho e il Real, che ci contavano, si sono ritrovati un ragazzo che ha preferito restare a Como.

Quando il Real attiva la recompra, il Como rimane in silenzio nonostante la tempesta mediatica di circa 24 ore. Si parlava tanto dell'Inter: l'Inter avrebbe voluto provarci ma lui è stato molto onesto dicendo qeste parole: "Io voglio stare al Como". Quel famoso discorso Inter è morto ancora prima di cominciare perché tutto ruotava attorno al Como che ha mantenuto la sua tranquillità forte dell'ok del giocatore”. Ora se lo gode il Como, che arriverà a San Siro proprio contro i nerazzurri per la prossima giornata di Serie A.

Vom Voxel-Stadtplan zum begehbaren Protein: Was Warschau in Voxeln Entwickler:innen über immersive Visualisierung lehrt

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Auf Hacker News trendet derzeit ein interaktives 3D-Modell von Warschaus Zentrum – Voxel für Voxel gebaut, flüssig zoombar vom Stadtüberblick bis zur Kreuzung (212 Punkte, 36 Kommentare). Das Projekt demonstriert: Wenn Daten räumlich begehbar werden, entstehen neue Erkenntnisse, die flache Projektionen verbergen.

Ich saß vor einem halben Jahr in meinem kleinen Home‑Office in Leipzig, der zweite Monitor zeigte ein PDB‑Bandmodell von KRAS‑G12D. Die Taschenvolume, die elektrostatischen Felder, die Allosterie‑Wege – alles flach, alles „nur“ 2‑D. Ich erinnerte mich an den Warschau‑Voxel‑Stadtplan, den ich morgens auf HN gesehen hatte: Man zoomt vom Stadtbild direkt in eine Kreuzung, sieht Verkehrsströme, Fußgängerwege, Ampelphasen. Warum nicht dieselbe Idee für Proteine?

Genau da stieß ich auf NexoPeek (Meta Quest, Seven Sportz). Die App verspricht, den Voxel‑Zoom in die Strukturbiologie zu bringen. Ich lud sie herunter, zog die Quest‑Brille auf und war sofort in einem Raum, in dem ein 3‑D‑Modell von KRAS schwebte – nicht als Video, sondern als interaktive Szene, die ich mit den Controllern drehen, skalieren und in die Tiefe „hineinzoomen“ konnte.

Zwölf Touren, gegliedert in sechs Säulen (Biotech‑Grundlagen, Grundlagen des Lebens, Signalwege, Medikamente, Gentechnik, Grüne Biotech), basieren allesamt auf echten PDB‑Strukturen. Jede Tour nutzt Spatial Audio und interaktive 3‑D‑Szenen – keine vorgerenderten Videos. Für mich fühlte sich das an wie ein persönlicher Mentor, der mich durch die Bindetasche führt, während ich die Residuen per „Residue‑Pill“ antippe und sofort ein 3‑D‑Halo sehe.

Smart‑Search & Multi‑Source Resolution Ein einziges Suchfeld löst Gene/UniProt‑IDs über RCSB PDB, UniProtKB, AlphaFold DB, SWISS‑MODEL, PDBe auf. Synonyme werden automatisch aufgelöst, Modell‑Validierung läuft im Hintergrund. Ich tippte „KRAS“ und erhielt innerhalb von Sekunden die AlphaFold‑Vorhersage, die experimentelle Struktur (1XK5) und ein homologes Modell aus SWISS‑MODEL – alles versioniert und bereit für den Vergleich.

Local Ingestion Pipeline Unterstützte Formate: .pdb, .cif, .mol2, .gro, .xyz, .dx, .xtc, .py. Overlays wie elektrostatische Feldlinien, Custom‑Bond‑Definitionen und Elektronendichte‑Volumen lassen sich per Drag‑&‑Drop hinzufügen. Ich lud eine eigene MD‑Trajektorie (.xtc) und sah die Konformationsänderung der Switch‑I‑Region in Echtzeit.

Mol*-Presets (sechs One‑Tap) Cartoon, Ball‑and‑Stick, Spacefill CPK, Surface, B‑Factor Heat, Tube Trace – alles MolViewSpec‑konform, ein Tap. Kein manuelles Scripting nötig.

Granular Sequence & Chain Control Per‑Chain AA‑Scroll, Residue‑Pills → 3‑D‑Halo, Custom‑Atom‑Pinning, Chain‑Isolation. Ich isolierte Kette A, pinnte das katalytische Lysin und verfolgte die Wasserstoffbrücken zum gebundenen GTP‑Analogon.

Mixed Reality Passthrough Room View lässt die Struktur im physischen Raum schweben; das Wall‑Menü ist per Handsteuerung bedienbar. Kein Medizinprodukt – die Analyse‑Tools unterstützen, ersetzen aber keine validierten Pipelines.

Der Warschau‑Voxel‑Plan zeigte mir, dass räumliche Navigation nicht nur „schön“ ist, sondern Entscheidungen beschleunigt. In NexoPeek übersetzt sich das in:

Ein kleiner Code‑Snippet, den ich für den Export nutze:

Damit lässt sich der gesamte Analyse‑Stand in CI/CD‑Pipelines einbinden – ein echter Gewinn für Teams, die FAIR‑Prinzipien auch in der immersiven Visualisierung leben wollen.

Als ich die erste Pocket‑Prediction per Offline‑Engine sah, war ich skeptisch: „Kann ein Headset wirklich die Genauigkeit eines Cluster‑Jobs liefern?“ Die Antwort kam prompt: Die vorhergesagte Tasche deckte sich zu 96 % mit der kristallographisch bestimmten Bindetasche (RMSD ≈ 0.8 Å). Ich konnte die Ergebnisse direkt in unserem internen Docking‑Workflow weiternutzen – ohne Dateikonvertierung, ohne Kontextverlust.

Seither nutze ich NexoPeek nicht nur für Demos, sondern als täglichen Begleiter bei der Struktur‑basierten Drug‑Discovery. Die Mischung aus immersiver Exploration und harten, exportierbaren Daten hat meinen Workflow verändert: Ich sehe nicht mehr nur Bänder, ich bewege mich durch das Protein.

Wenn du neugierig bist, wie sich Voxel‑Zoom auf deine eigenen Strukturdaten anfühlt, probier es selbst aus.

This isn't a bug. It's the default.

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 4 • Originally published at randomchaos.us

A machine running OpenAI's Codex wrote about 37 TB to its main SSD in 21 days of uptime. Process and file-level checks pinned the continuous writer to one place: Codex's local SQLite feedback log at ~/.codex/logs_2.sqlite. At that rate the drive takes roughly 640 TB a year, about 640 full-drive writes on a 1 TB SSD. Many consumer drives are rated near 600 TBW, so Codex alone can burn a drive's warranted write endurance in under a year.

The database looks small. It retains about half a million rows and sits around 1.2 GiB. The SQLite AUTOINCREMENT counter tells the real story: it has advanced past 5.5 billion ids, a roughly 10,000x gap between rows kept and rows ever inserted. In one 15-second sample about 36,211 rows went in while the retained count never moved. Codex inserts a batch, indexes it, writes it to the WAL, then prunes inside the same transaction. The file size stays flat and the disk keeps taking writes, which is why drive wear rather than database growth is the first visible signal.

The cause is a single line. The persistent SQLite sink is attached with a global TRACE default, Targets::new().with_default(Level::TRACE), in both the app-server and the TUI. Every target gets persisted at TRACE, so the database fills with logs no one chose to keep. The default log level, not a stray code path, is what writes the terabytes, and every install ships this way.

The high-frequency rows are dependency noise and raw protocol traffic: inotify events (128,764 OPEN events for ld.so.cache, 23,843 for passwd, more for nsswitch.conf and locale.alias), thousands of tokio-tungstenite poll_read and with_context entries, websocket frame fields, and repeated OpenTelemetry mirror rows from codex_otel.log_only and codex_otel.trace_safe. TRACE alone is 70.7% of retained bytes; the two OTel mirror targets add another 25.3%. Dropping those categories removes about 96% of retained log bytes without turning feedback logging off. Raw websocket and SSE payload bodies are also persisted, and the reporter left them out of the public sample because they can contain private conversation content.

A large logs DB does more than wear the SSD. On startup the app-server's SQLite pool can time out (#27741), and a 419 MB logs DB alongside session state has produced 1-to-5-minute cold starts on WSL2 (#28166). One commenter reports that Codex in /goal mode will delete files and folders trying to free space when the disk is full, so a full drive becomes data loss, and a Linux box rebooted while full can fail to log in.

Three PRs merged by late June 2026 and, per the reporter's own Codex, cut persisted logs by about 85%. #29432 stops logging every Responses websocket event and #29457 filters noisy targets, both in 0.142.0; #29599 stops persisting bridged log events in 0.143.0. OpenAI says the series of fixes is in the latest shipping desktop app, CLI, and IDE extension.

Not all of it has shipped. A later comment notes 0.144.1 is missing #31789 through #31792, which appear in 0.145.0-alpha.4 and presumably 0.145.0. A separate root cause, tracked in #32496, has Codex continually rewriting models_cache.json and scope_v3.json for roughly another 1.3 TB a year, well under the logging figure but still idle I/O for nothing. Testing with GPT 5.6 Sol High on macOS, one commenter measured unmitigated churn falling from 33.1 to 7.36 inserted ids per second after the upstream fixes, with a local filter taking it to 0.45.

On a build without the fixes, quit Codex and install a trigger that rejects log inserts outright:

That blocks every insert, feedback logging included, but it stops the writes at once. Several people who built working filter patches could not upstream them: GitHub returned "does not have the correct permissions to execute CreatePullRequest" on their accounts, so the real fixes waited on maintainers either way.

Before and after slider in React, accessible and draggable

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 4 • Originally published at facadeui.com

Roofers, painters, landscapers and remodelers all have the same proof: a photo from before the job and a photo from after. Put the two side by side and the eye wanders. Put them in one frame with a handle the visitor drags and the eye follows the handle. That is why the comparison slider earns a spot on every home services site I build, and why I spent more time on the Before / after slider in Facade than its size suggests. Most of that time went into things you cannot see in a demo: touch, keyboard and screen readers.

The first version of every comparison slider on the internet listens to mousedown, mousemove and mouseup. Then someone opens it on a phone and nothing happens, so touchstart and touchmove get bolted on, and now there are two code paths that drift apart.

Pointer events replace both. onPointerDown, onPointerMove and onPointerUp fire for mouse, touch and pen with the same event shape, and clientX means the same thing in all three. The one extra call that makes dragging reliable is setPointerCapture: once the visitor presses down, the element keeps receiving move events even when the finger or cursor leaves its box. Without it, a fast drag that exits the frame freezes the handle halfway.

fromClientX reads the container's bounding rect and converts the pointer position into a percentage between 0 and 100. That percentage is the only piece of state. Everything else, the clip, the handle position, the ARIA attributes, derives from it.

Listen on the whole frame, not just the handle. Visitors tap anywhere on the photo and expect the handle to jump there. A 24 pixel handle as the only hit target is a usability bug on touch screens.

The after photo fills the frame. The before photo sits on top of it in an absolutely positioned layer, and clip-path: inset(0 X% 0 0) cuts that layer off on the right at the handle position. Dragging changes X and the before photo reveals or hides the after photo underneath.

Clip-path is the right tool because the two images never move or resize. A common alternative is to change the width of the top layer, which stretches the image unless you also counter-scale it, and that path leads to subpixel jitter during a drag. With clip-path both images stay pixel aligned and the browser only repaints the clipped region.

In the Facade component the value runs through a useSpring from motion/react and useTransform turns the spring into the clip string. The trick is in one effect: while dragging, the spring jumps straight to the value so the handle follows the finger one to one; when the visitor releases or taps somewhere, the spring eases to the new position. Reduced motion users get the jump in both cases.

A slider that only works with a pointer is a slider half the audience cannot use. Keyboard support is a single handler on the focusable handle, and it follows the conventions people already know from range inputs:

Call preventDefault on the keys you handle so the page does not scroll when someone presses Down with the handle focused. Do not handle keys you do not use; Tab must keep working.

Screen reader support is where I see the most comparison sliders fail, and it is not much work. The handle is the one element in the tab order, and it carries:

The two images are not decorative. When you pass beforeSrc and afterSrc, each layer renders an with the beforeAlt and afterAlt text, because "Roof before replacement, missing shingles on the south side" is information a sighted visitor gets from the photo. The illustrated fallback that renders without URLs uses role="img" with the same labels.

Three things break comparison sliders on phones, and all three are fixable with CSS and one attribute.

First, scrolling. A horizontal drag on a touch screen is also a scroll gesture, and the browser will happily scroll the page while you try to move the handle. touch-action: none on the frame tells the browser that this element owns its touches. Only set it on the slider, never on the page.

Second, selection. A long press selects the image or the labels. user-select: none on the frame prevents it.

Third, the handle size. The visual handle can be slim, but the hit area is the entire frame thanks to the pointer handlers above, so a thumb never has to land on a two pixel line. The handle itself still gets a 44 pixel circle so it reads as something to grab.

The slider is a Pro item and installs from the private registry once your license key is in components.json (one time setup in the installation docs):

Shoot the two photos from the same spot with the same lens, or the reveal shows a misaligned house instead of a transformed roof. A tripod mark on the driveway is enough. On the roofer page the slider sits directly under the hero, before the stats, because a roofer's work is the proof and the proof should come before the claims.

Yes. Empty room before, staged room after, same camera position. It works for renovations, landscaping, dental smile makeovers and anything else with a credible before state. It does not work for stock photos, and visitors can tell.

The beforeSrc and afterSrc props take plain URLs and render standard images. If you need next/image for sizing and formats, the source is yours after installation; swap the two elements and keep the alt text in place.

The frame uses aspect-ratio so both photos share one box regardless of their native size, and 16 by 10 fits most exterior and interior shots without cropping the roofline. Override it with a Tailwind aspect-* class through className.
